, recall from Remark
11
that we write
P_{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{j\times j}
for the matrix that represents the orthogonal projection
\Pi_{\mathcal{L}_{A}}
onto
\mathcal{L}_{A}=\Span\{u^{\ell^{\prime}}:\ell^{\prime}\in A\cup\{0\}\}
, the subspace consisting of all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{j}
whose knots lie in
A
.

Proof of Lemma
10
.

For each
\tilde{v}\in\{v^{\prime},v^{\prime\prime}\}
, we have
\Pi_{\Lambda}(\tilde{v})\in F_{A}\subseteq\Img(U_{A})
, and Lemma
9
implies that
\langle u^{\ell},\tilde{v}-\Pi_{\Lambda}(\tilde{v})\rangle=0
for all
\ell\in A\cup\{0\}
. This shows that
P_{A}(\tilde{v})=\Pi_{\Lambda}(\tilde{v})\in\relint F_{A}
for
\tilde{v}\in\{v^{\prime},v^{\prime\prime}\}
. Now fix
t\in[0,1]
and
v:=(1-t)v^{\prime}+tv^{\prime\prime}
. Then
P_{A}(v)=(1-t)P_{A}(v^{\prime})+tP_{A}(v^{\prime\prime})=(1-t)\Pi_{\Lambda}(v^{\prime})+t\Pi_{\Lambda}(v^{\prime\prime})\in\relint F_{A}
by the convexity of
\relint F_{A}
. In addition, by applying Lemma
9
to
\Pi_{\Lambda}(v^{\prime}),\Pi_{\Lambda}(v^{\prime\prime})
, we deduce that

\langle u^{\ell},v-P_{A}(v)\rangle=(1-t)\langle u^{\ell},v^{\prime}-\Pi_{\Lambda}(v^{\prime})\rangle+t\langle u^{\ell},v^{\prime\prime}-\Pi_{\Lambda}(v^{\prime\prime})\rangle\leq 0

for all
0\leq\ell\leq j-1
, with equality if
\ell\in A\cup\{0\}
(in view of the definition of
P_{A}
). It follows from Lemma
9
that
\Pi_{\Lambda}(v)=P_{A}(v)\in\relint F_{A}
, as required.
∎

Proof of Lemma
12
.

For (iv), we know from (iii) that
\Pi_{\Lambda^{j}}\bigl(v(t)\bigr)=P_{A_{r}}v(t)
for all
t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1}]
after an iteration of (II). By applying Lemma
10
, we deduce that there exist
\eta>0
and
A^{\prime}\subseteq[j-1]
with
A^{\prime}\neq A_{r}
such that
\hat{v}(t):=\Pi_{\Lambda^{j}}\bigl(v(t)\bigr)=P_{A^{\prime}}v(t)\in\Lambda^{j}\cap\mathcal{L}_{A^{\prime}}=\{u\in\Lambda^{j}:A(u)\subseteq A^{\prime}\}
for all
t\in[t_{r+1},t_{r+1}+\eta]
. Now in (IV), note that

•

If
\ell\in A_{r}\setminus A_{r}^{-}
, then
\lambda_{\ell}\bigl(\hat{v}(t_{r+1})\bigr)=\beta_{\ell}(t_{r+1})>0
, so
\ell\in A\bigl(\hat{v}(t_{r+1})\bigr)\subseteq A^{\prime}
;

•

If
\ell\in A^{\prime}
, then
\gamma_{\ell}(t_{r+1})=\langle u^{\ell},v(t_{r+1})-\hat{v}(t_{r+1})\rangle=\langle u^{\ell},(I-P_{A^{\prime}})v(t_{r+1})\rangle=0
, so
\ell\in\{1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq j-1:\gamma_{\ell^{\prime}}(t_{r+1})=0\}=A_{r}\cup A_{r}^{+}
.

Thus,
A_{r}\setminus A_{r}^{-}\subseteq A^{\prime}\subseteq A_{r}\cup A_{r}^{+}
, so in all cases, (IV) is guaranteed to find subsets
A^{\pm}\subseteq A_{r}^{\pm}
such that when we take
A_{r+1}=(A_{r}\setminus A^{-})\cup A^{+}
, the next iteration of (II) strictly increases
t
. In particular, this always happens in scenario (a) where
\lvert A_{r}^{-}\cup A_{r}^{+}\rvert=1
,
since we necessarily have
A^{\prime}=(A_{r}\setminus A_{r}^{-})\cup A_{r}^{+}=A_{r+1}
in this case.

For (v), recall again that for each
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have
\Pi_{\Lambda^{j}}\bigl(v(t)\bigr)=P_{A_{r}}v(t)\equiv\hat{v}_{r}(t)
for all
t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1}]
. As noted in (II),
\lambda_{\ell}\bigl(\hat{v}_{r}(t)\bigr)
and
\langle u^{\ell},v(t)-\hat{v}_{r}(t)\rangle
vary linearly with
t
for all
\ell\in[j-1]
, so it follows from this and (
18
) that

t_{r+1}=\sup\,\bigl\{t\geq t_{r}:\lambda_{\ell}\bigl(\hat{v}_{r}(t)\bigr)\geq 0,\,\langle u^{\ell},v(t)-\hat{v}_{r}(t)\rangle\leq 0\text{ for all }1\leq\ell\leq j-1\bigr\}.

Thus, by Lemma
9
, we cannot have
\hat{v}_{r}(t)=\Pi_{\Lambda^{j}}\bigl(v(t)\bigr)
for any
t>t_{r+1}
, so
A_{r}\neq A_{r^{\prime}}
for all
r^{\prime}>r
, as claimed.
∎

Degeneracies in Algorithm
7
: It can be verified that there is a set
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{j}
of Lebesgue measure 0 such that if
(v(0),v(1))\notin A
, then no degeneracies occur on the trajectory of the algorithm. Thus, degeneracies are rarely an issue when
v(0),v(1)
are obtained from simulated or real data rather than artificially constructed (see Example
S19
). To avoid them in practice,
Fraser and Massam 1989
and
Meyer 1999
suggest slightly perturbing
v(0),v(1)
or some intermediate
v(t_{r})
. The approach we outline in Stage (IV) covers all eventualities in the degenerate scenario (b), but this can be time-consuming when
\lvert A_{r}^{-}\cup A_{r}^{+}\rvert
is large.

In the special case where
u=v(0)-v(1)
is a positive multiple of
u^{j-1}=e_{j}=(0,\dotsc,0,1)\in\mathbb{R}^{j}
(which is of particular relevance in the
SeqConReg
procedure in Section
3
), a more efficient alternative to (IV) is as follows:

(IV’)

Instead define
A_{r}^{-}:=\{\ell\in A_{r}:\beta_{\ell}(t_{r+1})=0,\,\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}^{A_{r}}(u)>0\}
and
A_{r}^{+}:=\{\ell\in A_{r}^{c}:\gamma_{\ell}(t_{r+1})=0,\,\hat{\zeta}_{\ell}^{A_{r}}(u)<0\}
, and let
\ell_{\max}:=\max(A_{r}^{-}\cup A_{r}^{+})
. Let

A_{r+1}:=A_{r}\setminus\{\ell_{\max}\}
if
\ell_{\max}\in A_{r}^{-}
and otherwise let
A_{r+1}:=A_{r}\cup\{\ell_{\max}\}
if
\ell_{\max}\in A_{r}^{+}
. Then execute (II) and (III) with this
A_{r+1}
(and
r+1
in place of
r
throughout).

If there is a degeneracy at
t_{r+1}
, then when we run this modified algorithm, there may be several subsequent iterations of (II) in which
t
does not increase (i.e. we remain at
t_{r+1}
). Nevertheless, the choice of
\ell_{\max}=\max(A_{r}^{-}\cup A_{r}^{+})
in (IV’) ensures that property (iv) still holds, and hence that the algorithm terminates with the exact solution (usually after fewer iterations than in the original).

Proposition S18
.

Suppose that
u=v(0)-v(1)
is a positive multiple of
u^{j-1}=e_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{j}
. Then with modification (IV’), Algorithm
7
terminates with the correct solution after finitely many steps, and the following hold for any
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

(a)

\max A_{r}\geq\max A_{r+1}
; in other words, if
\max A_{r}<\ell\leq j-1
, then
\ell\notin A_{r^{\prime}}
for any
r^{\prime}>r
.

(b)

Let
\ell_{r}:=\max(\{\ell\in A_{r}:\ell+1\in A_{r}\}\cup\{0\})
. Then in (
19
), we have
\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}^{A_{r}}(u)\equiv\lambda_{\ell}(P_{A_{r}}u)=0
for all
0\leq\ell\leq\ell_{r}-1
and
\hat{\zeta}_{\ell}^{A_{r}}(u)\equiv\langle u^{\ell},(I-P_{A_{r}})u\rangle=0
for all
0\leq\ell\leq\ell_{r}+1
.

This follows from Lemma
S20
below, which captures some specific structural features of the generators
\pm u^{0},u^{1},\dotsc,u^{j-1}
of
\Lambda^{j}
. The facts in (a) and (b) lead to some additional computational shortcuts in Algorithm
7
when
u
is a positive multiple of
e_{j}
. Specifically, when computing
t_{r+1}
in Stage (II) of the procedure, it follows from Proposition
S18
that we need only compute the ratios in (
19
) for
\ell_{r}<\ell\leq\max A_{r}
. Thus, when
t\geq t_{r}
, we can drop all
\beta_{\ell}(t)
and
\gamma_{\ell}(t)
with
\ell>\max A_{r}
, and when updating the primal and dual variables for use in subsequent iterations, no calculations are needed to see that
\beta_{\ell}(t_{r+1})=\beta_{\ell}(t_{r})
and
\gamma_{\ell}(t_{r+1})=\gamma_{\ell}(t_{r})
for all
1\leq\ell\leq\ell_{r}
.

Example S19
.

We can actually write down explicitly the sequence of ‘active sets’
A_{0},A_{1},\dotsc
obtained by Algorithm
7
in the special case where
v(0)\in\Lambda^{j}
and
u=v(0)-v(1)
is a positive multiple of
e_{j}
. This can happen if for example in (
7
), the observations
Y_{1},\dotsc,Y_{j}
are drawn according to a
noiseless
regression model (
2
) in which
f_{0}
is increasing and convex on
[x_{1},x_{j-1}]
. With
A_{0}=A\bigl(v(0)\bigr)
, it turns out that for
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have

A_{r+1}=\begin{cases}A_{r}\setminus\{\max A_{r}\}&\quad\text{if }\max A_{r}-1\in A_{r}\text{ or }\max A_{r}=j-1\\
A_{r}\cup\{\max A_{r}-1\}&\quad\text{if }\max A_{r}-1\notin A_{r}\text{ and }\max A_{r}<j-1.\end{cases}

(S35)

Indeed, given that
v(0)\in\Lambda^{j}
and hence that
\gamma_{\ell}(0)=0
for all
0\leq\ell\leq j-1
, we can apply Proposition
S18
to establish inductively that
\gamma_{\ell}(t_{r+1})=0
for all
0\leq\ell\leq\max A_{r}
and
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. In particular, we always have
A_{r}^{+}=\{1,\dotsc,\max A_{r}-1\}\cap A_{r}^{c}
, and

•

If
\max A_{r}-1\in A_{r}
or
\max A_{r}=j-1
, then
t_{r}<t_{r+1}
and
A_{r}^{-}=\{\max A_{r}\}
;

•

If
\max A_{r}-1\notin A_{r}
and
\max A_{r}<j-1
, then
t_{r}=t_{r+1}
and
A_{r}^{-}=\emptyset
.

Note that unless
\{1,\dotsc,\max A_{r}-2\}\subseteq A_{r}
, there is a degeneracy at
t_{r+1}
, so we use (IV’) above to form the next ‘active set’
A_{r+1}
. In addition, we have
\max A_{r}>\max A_{r+2}
for all
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
in view of (
S35
), so the number of distinct ‘active sets’ on the trajectory of Algorithm
7
is at most
2(j-1)
.
This is much less than
2^{j-1}
, the total number of subsets of
[j-1]
, and an open question is whether for general
v(0)\in\mathbb{R}^{j}
(and
u=v(0)-v(1)
as above), the number of ‘active sets’ is necessarily bounded above by a polynomial in
j
. If this is always true (or true in ‘most’ cases), then our sequential procedure for increasing convex regression is guaranteed to have a worst-case (or average-case) complexity that is at most polynomial in the number of observations
n
.

For fixed
j\in[n]
,
\ell\in[j-1]
and
A\subseteq[j-1]
, Lemma
S20
determines the signs of the entries of
(I-P_{A})u^{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{j}
indexed by
A\cup\{\ell\}
. This yields useful information on how the primal and dual variables change in Algorithm
7
when the vector
u=v(0)-v(1)
therein is a positive multiple of
e_{j}
. This enables us to justify the more efficient implementation (IV’) of Stage (IV) of this procedure, as well as assertions (a) and (b) in Proposition
S18
on the composition of the resulting active sets.

We write
e_{1},\dotsc,e_{j}
for the standard basis vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{j}
and
\langle\cdot\,,\cdot\rangle
for the standard Euclidean inner product. For
t\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\sgn(t):=(\lvert t\rvert/t)\mathbbm{1}_{\{t\neq 0\}}
.

Lemma S20
.

For
A\subseteq[j-1]
, enumerate the elements of
A
as
a_{1}>a_{2}>\cdots>a_{m}
, and let
a_{0}=j
and
a_{m+1}=0
. Fix
\ell\in[j-1]
. Then
(I-P_{A})u^{\ell}\in\mathcal{L}_{A\cup\{\ell\}}
, and
(I-P_{A})u^{\ell}=0
if and only if
\ell\in A
.

Suppose now that
\ell\notin A
and let
q\in[m+1]
be such that
a_{q}<\ell<a_{q-1}
. Define
q_{-}:=\max(\{1\leq\tilde{q}\leq q-1:a_{\tilde{q}-1}=a_{\tilde{q}}+1\}\cup\{0\})
and
q_{+}:=\min(\{q+1\leq\tilde{q}\leq m:a_{\tilde{q}-1}=a_{\tilde{q}}+1\}\cup\{m+1\})
. Then
\langle(I-P_{A})u^{\ell},e_{\ell}\rangle<0
,
\langle P_{A}u^{\ell},e_{1}\rangle=\langle P_{A}u^{\ell},e_{a_{m}}\rangle
, and for
0\leq s\leq m
, we have

\sgn\bigl(\langle(I-P_{A})u^{\ell},e_{a_{s}}\rangle\bigr)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{s-q}&\quad\text{if }q\leq s<q_{+}\\
(-1)^{q-1-s}&\quad\text{if }q_{-}\leq s\leq q-1\\
0&\quad\text{if }s<q_{+}\text{ or }s\geq q_{+}.\end{cases}

(S36)

Proof of Lemma
S20
.

The generators
u^{0},u^{1},\dotsc,u^{j-1}
of the cone
\Lambda^{j}
are linearly independent, so
u^{\ell}\equiv(u_{1}^{\ell},\dotsc,u_{j}^{\ell})=\bigl((x_{i}-x_{\ell})^{+}:1\leq i\leq j\bigr)\in\mathcal{L}_{A}
(i.e.
(I-P_{A})u^{\ell}=0
) if and only if
\ell\in A
. Suppose henceforth that
\ell\notin A
and let
\tilde{z}\equiv(\tilde{z}_{1},\dotsc,\tilde{z}_{j}):=P_{A}u^{\ell}=\argmin_{z\in\mathcal{L}_{A}}\|u^{\ell}-z\|
. Then
u^{\ell}-\tilde{z}=(I-P_{A})u^{\ell}\in\Span(\{u^{\ell}\}\cup\mathcal{L}_{A})=\mathcal{L}_{A\cup\{\ell\}}
, so
u^{\ell}-\tilde{z}
is determined by
\{u_{i}^{\ell}-\tilde{z}_{i}=\langle(I-P_{A})u^{\ell},e_{i}\rangle:i\in A\cup\{\ell\}\}
.

To establish (
S36
), we make the following additional definitions. For
z\in\mathbb{R}^{j}
and
J=\{b,b+1,\dotsc,b^{\prime}\}
with
1\leq b\leq b^{\prime}\leq j
, we write
z_{J}=(z_{b},z_{b+1},\dotsc,z_{b^{\prime}})
for the subvector of
z
indexed by
J
. For
s\in[m]
, partition
[j]
into the subsets

J_{s}^{+}:=\{1,\dotsc,a_{s}\},\quad J_{s}:=\{a_{s}+1,\dotsc,a_{s-1}-1\},\quad J_{s}^{-}:=\{a_{s-1},\dotsc,j\},

and for
i\in J_{s}
, let
t_{i}^{J_{s}}:=(x_{i}-x_{a_{s}})/(x_{a_{s-1}}-x_{a_{s}})\in(0,1)
, so that any affine sequence based on
(x_{i}:i\in J_{s})
can be written in the form
v^{J_{s}}(\lambda,\vartheta):=\bigl((1-t_{i}^{J_{s}})(u_{a_{s}}^{\ell}-\lambda)+t_{i}^{J_{s}}(u_{a_{s-1}}^{\ell}-\vartheta):a_{s}+1\leq i\leq a_{s-1}-1\bigr)
for some
\lambda,\vartheta\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover,

\mathcal{L}_{A,s}^{+}(\lambda):=\{z_{J_{s}^{+}}:z\in\mathcal{L}_{A},\,z_{a_{s}}=\lambda\}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\lvert J_{s}^{+}\rvert}\;\text{ and }\;\mathcal{L}_{A,s}^{-}(\lambda):=\{z_{J_{s}^{-}}:z\in\mathcal{L}_{A},\,z_{a_{s-1}}=\lambda\}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\lvert J_{s}^{-}\rvert}

are affine subspaces for each
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{A,s}^{\pm}(\lambda)=\lambda\mathcal{L}_{A,s}^{\pm}(1)
if
\lambda\neq 0
, so the vectors
\tilde{v}^{J_{s}^{\pm}}:=\argmin_{v\in\mathcal{L}_{A,s}^{\pm}(1)}\|v\|
and
\lambda\tilde{v}^{J_{s}^{\pm}}=\argmin_{v\in\mathcal{L}_{A,s}^{\pm}(\lambda)}\|v\|
are well-defined for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
.

Claim 1
.

Let
\star\in\{+,-\}
and
s\in[m-1]
be such that
a_{s},a_{s\star 1}\in J_{q}^{\star}
. If
\lvert a_{s}-a_{s\star 1}\rvert=1
, then
\tilde{v}_{a_{s\star 1}}^{J_{q}^{\star}}=0
. Otherwise, if
\lvert a_{s}-a_{s\star 1}\rvert>1
, then
\sgn\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s\star 1}}^{J_{q}^{\star}}\bigr)=-\sgn\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{\star}}\bigr)
. Thus,

\sgn\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{s-q}&\quad\text{if }q\leq s<q_{+}\\
0&\quad\text{if }q_{+}\leq s\leq m;\end{cases}\quad\sgn\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{-}}\bigr)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{q-1-s}&\quad\text{if }q_{-}\leq s\leq q-1\\
0&\quad\text{if }0\leq s<q_{-}.\end{cases}

Proof of Claim
1
.

We focus on the case
\star=+
; the arguments for
\star=-
are similar. Note that
a_{s},a_{s+1}\in J_{s}^{+}
precisely when
q\leq s\leq m-1
. For any such
s
and
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
, define
\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}(s;\mu)\equiv\bigl(\tilde{v}_{i}^{J_{q}^{+}}(s,\mu):1\leq i\leq a_{q}\bigr)
by

\tilde{v}_{i}^{J_{q}^{+}}(s;\mu):=\begin{cases}\mu\tilde{v}_{i}^{J_{s+1}^{+}}=\argmin_{v\in\mathcal{L}_{A,s+1}^{+}(\mu)}\|v\|\;\>&\text{for }i\in J_{s+1}^{+}=\{1,\dotsc,a_{s+1}\}\\
(1-t_{i}^{J_{s+1}})\mu+t_{i}^{J_{s+1}}\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}=v_{i}^{J_{s+1}}\bigl(-\mu,-\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)\;\>&\text{for }i\in J_{s+1}\\
\tilde{v}_{i}^{J_{q}^{+}}\;\>&\text{for }i\in J_{s+1}^{-}\cap J_{q}^{+}=\{a_{s},\dotsc,a_{q}\}.\end{cases}

Then
\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}(s;\mu)=\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}
, and since
\mu\tilde{v}^{J_{s+1}^{+}}\in\mathcal{L}_{A,s+1}^{+}(\mu)
, we have
\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}(s;\mu)=\mu
, so
\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}(s;\mu)\in\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{+}(1)
. Observe in addition that
\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigl(s;\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)=\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}
; indeed,
\tilde{v}_{i}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigl(s;\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)=\tilde{v}_{i}^{J_{q}^{+}}
for
a_{s+1}+1\leq i\leq a_{q}
and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{A,q,s+1}^{+}

\displaystyle:=\bigl\{v\equiv(v_{1},\dotsc,v_{a_{q}})\in\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{+}:v_{i}=\tilde{v}_{i}^{J_{q}^{+}}\text{ for }a_{s+1}+1\leq i\leq a_{q}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=\bigl\{(v_{1},\dotsc,v_{a_{q}})\in\mathbb{R}^{a_{q}}:v_{J_{s+1}^{+}}\in\mathcal{L}_{A,s+1}^{+}\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr),\,v_{i}=\tilde{v}_{i}^{J_{q}^{+}}\text{ for }a_{s+1}+1\leq i\leq a_{q}\bigr\},

so
\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}=\argmin_{v\in\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{+}(1)}\|v\|=\argmin_{v\in\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{A,q,s+1}^{+}}\!\|v\|
satisfies

(\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}})_{J_{s+1}^{+}}=\argmin_{v^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{A,s+1}^{+}\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)}\!\|v^{\prime}\|=\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\tilde{v}^{J_{s+1}^{+}}=\bigl(\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}(s;\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}})\bigr)_{J_{s+1}^{+}}.

Thus,

\mu\mapsto r_{s}(\mu):=\|\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}(s;\mu)\|^{2}=\mu^{2}\,\|\tilde{v}^{J_{s+1}^{+}}\|^{2}+\sum_{i\in J_{s+1}}\bigl((1-t_{i}^{J_{s+1}})\mu+t_{i}^{J_{s+1}}\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)^{2}+\sum_{i=a_{s}}^{a_{q}}\bigl(\tilde{v}_{i}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)^{2}

is a quadratic function with
r_{s}\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)=\|\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}\|^{2}=\min_{v\in\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{+}(1)}\|v\|^{2}=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}r_{s}(\mu)
, so

0=r_{s}^{\prime}\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)=2\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\,\|\tilde{v}^{J_{s+1}^{+}}\|^{2}+2\sum_{i\in J_{s+1}}\bigl((1-t_{i}^{J_{s+1}})\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}+t_{i}^{J_{s+1}}\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)\bigl(1-t_{i}^{J_{s+1}}\bigr).

(S37)

Now
\|\tilde{v}^{J_{s+1}^{+}}\|^{2}>0
by the definition of
\tilde{v}^{J_{s+1}^{+}}\in\mathcal{L}_{A,s+1}^{+}(1)
, so if
a_{s}=a_{s+1}+1
, i.e.
J_{s+1}=\emptyset
, then
\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}=0
by (
S37
). On the other hand, suppose instead that
a_{s}>a_{s+1}+1
, in which case
J_{s+1}\neq\emptyset
. If
\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}=0
, then
\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}=0
by (
S37
). When
\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}>0
, we must have
\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}<0
, since otherwise the first term on the right-hand side of (
S37
) would be non-negative and each summand in the second term would be strictly positive, contradicting the fact that
r_{s}^{\prime}\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)=0
. Similarly, if
\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}<0
, then
\tilde{v}_{a_{s+1}}^{J_{q}^{+}}>0
. This completes the proof of the claim.
∎

Next, note that
u_{J_{q}^{+}}^{\ell}=0\in\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{+}(0)=\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{+}(u_{a_{q}}^{\ell})
and
u_{J_{q}^{-}}^{\ell}=(x_{i}-x_{\ell}:a_{q-1}\leq i\leq j)\in\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{-}(u_{a_{q-1}}^{\ell})
. Thus, for each
(\lambda,\vartheta)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{A}^{q}(\lambda,\vartheta)

\displaystyle:=\{z\in\mathcal{L}_{A}:(u^{\ell}-z)_{a_{q}}=\lambda,\,(u^{\ell}-z)_{a_{q-1}}=\vartheta\}

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=\bigl\{z\in\mathcal{L}_{A}:(u^{\ell}-z)_{J_{q}^{+}}\in\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{+}(\lambda),\,(u^{\ell}-z)_{J_{q}^{-}}\in\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{-}(\vartheta),\,z_{J_{q}}=v^{J_{q}}(\lambda,\vartheta)\bigr\},

(S38)

and the unique minimiser
\tilde{z}(\lambda,\vartheta)
of
z\mapsto\|u^{\ell}-z\|^{2}=\|(u^{\ell}-z)_{J_{q}^{+}}\|^{2}+\|(u^{\ell}-z)_{J_{q}}\|^{2}+\|(u^{\ell}-z)_{J_{q}^{-}}\|^{2}
over
\mathcal{L}_{A}^{q}(\lambda,\vartheta)
satisfies
\tilde{z}(\lambda,\vartheta)_{J_{q}}=v^{J_{q}}(\lambda,\vartheta)
,
\bigl(u^{\ell}-\tilde{z}(\lambda,\vartheta)\bigr)_{J_{q}^{+}}=\argmin_{v\in\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{+}(\lambda)}\|v\|=\lambda\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}
and
\bigl(u^{\ell}-\tilde{z}(\lambda,\vartheta)\bigr)_{J_{q}^{-}}=\argmin_{v\in\mathcal{L}_{A,q}^{-}(\vartheta)}\|v\|=\vartheta\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{-}}
. Let

\displaystyle r(\lambda,\vartheta)

\displaystyle:=\min_{z\in\mathcal{L}_{A}^{q}(\lambda,\vartheta)}\|u^{\ell}-z\|^{2}=\|u^{\ell}-\tilde{z}(\lambda,\vartheta)\|^{2}=\|\lambda\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}\|^{2}+\|u_{J_{q}}^{\ell}-v^{J_{q}}(\lambda,\vartheta)\|^{2}+\|\vartheta\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{-}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=\lambda^{2}\,\|\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}\|^{2}+\sum_{i\in J_{q}}\bigl(u_{i}^{\ell}-(1-t_{i}^{J_{q}})(u_{a_{q}}^{\ell}-\lambda)-t_{i}^{J_{q}}(u_{a_{q-1}}^{\ell}-\vartheta)\bigr)^{2}+\vartheta^{2}\,\|\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{-}}\|^{2},

so that
(\lambda,\vartheta)\mapsto r(\lambda,\vartheta)
is a quadratic form with

\nabla r(\lambda,\vartheta)=2\lambda\,\|\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}\|^{2}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}+2\sum_{i\in J_{q}}\bigl(u_{i}^{\ell}-v_{i}^{J_{q}}(\lambda,\vartheta)\bigr)\begin{pmatrix}1-t_{i}^{J_{q}}\\
t_{i}^{J_{q}}\end{pmatrix}+2\vartheta\,\|\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{-}}\|^{2}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}

(S39)

for each
(\lambda,\vartheta)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
\tilde{z}=P_{A}u^{\ell}
satisfies
\|u^{\ell}-\tilde{z}\|^{2}=\min_{z\in\mathcal{L}_{A}}\|u^{\ell}-z\|^{2}=\min_{(\lambda,\vartheta)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}r(\lambda,\vartheta)
. Thus, writing
\tilde{\lambda}:=(u^{\ell}-\tilde{z})_{a_{q}}=\langle(I-P_{A})u^{\ell},e_{a_{q}}\rangle
and
\tilde{\vartheta}:=(u^{\ell}-\tilde{z})_{a_{q-1}}=\langle(I-P_{A})u^{\ell},e_{a_{q-1}}\rangle
, we have
\tilde{z}=\tilde{z}(\tilde{\lambda},\tilde{\vartheta})
and
(\tilde{\lambda},\tilde{\vartheta})=\argmin_{(\lambda,\vartheta)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}r(\lambda,\vartheta)
,
whence
\nabla r(\tilde{\lambda},\tilde{\vartheta})=0
.

Claim 2
.

\tilde{\lambda},\tilde{\vartheta}>0
and
\langle(I-P_{A})u^{\ell},e_{\ell}\rangle=(u^{\ell}-\tilde{z})_{\ell}=u_{\ell}^{\ell}-v^{J_{q}}(\tilde{\lambda},\tilde{\vartheta})_{\ell}<0
.

Proof of Claim
2
.

It suffices to show that if
(\lambda,\vartheta)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
is such that either
\lambda\leq 0
,
\vartheta\leq 0
or
u_{\ell}^{\ell}-v^{J_{q}}(\lambda,\vartheta)_{\ell}\geq 0
, then
\nabla r(\lambda,\vartheta)\neq 0
. For any such
(\lambda,\vartheta)
, it is enough to prove that there exist
\lambda^{\prime},\vartheta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\lambda\lambda^{\prime}\geq 0
,
\vartheta\vartheta^{\prime}\geq 0
and
\bigl(u_{i}^{\ell}-v_{i}^{J_{q}}(\lambda,\vartheta)\bigr)\bigl((1-t_{i}^{J_{q}})\lambda^{\prime}+t_{i}^{J_{q}}\vartheta^{\prime}\bigr)\geq 0
for all
i\in J_{q}
, with at least one of these inequalities being strict, since then

\nabla r(\lambda,\vartheta)^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}\lambda^{\prime}\\
\vartheta^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}=2\lambda\lambda^{\prime}\,\|\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{+}}\|^{2}+2\sum_{i\in J_{q}}\bigl(u_{i}^{\ell}-v_{i}^{J_{q}}(\lambda,\vartheta)\bigr)\bigl((1-t_{i}^{J_{q}})\lambda^{\prime}+t_{i}^{J_{q}}\vartheta^{\prime}\bigr)+2\vartheta\vartheta^{\prime}\,\|\tilde{v}^{J_{q}^{-}}\|^{2}>0

by (
S39
). To this end, define the convex function
g\colon x\mapsto(x-x_{\ell})^{+}
on
[x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}}]
, and let
h
be the unique affine function with
h(x_{a_{q-1}})=g(x_{a_{q-1}})-\lambda
and
h(x_{a_{q}})=g(x_{a_{q}})-\vartheta
, so that
g(x_{i})=u_{i}^{\ell}
and
h(x_{i})=v_{i}^{J_{q}}(\lambda,\vartheta)
for
a_{q}\leq i\leq a_{q-1}
.
Since
(\lambda,\vartheta)
satisfies at least one of the three conditions above, the possibilities for
I:=\{x\in(x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}}):h(x)>g(x)\}
are as follows. In each case, we verify that there is an affine function
\tilde{h}
such that
\bigl(g(x)-h(x)\bigr)\bigl(h(x)-\tilde{h}(x)\bigr)\geq 0
for all
x\in[x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}}]
, with strict inequality for some
x\in\{x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q}+1},\dotsc,x_{a_{q-1}}\}
:

•

I=\emptyset
: then
g(x)\geq h(x)
for all
x\in[x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}}]
, and strict inequality holds for some
x\in\{x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q}+1},\dotsc,x_{a_{q-1}}\}
, so we can take
\tilde{h}
to be any affine function such that
\tilde{h}<h
on
[x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}}]
.

•

I=(x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}})
: by the continuity of
g,h
, we have
g(x)\leq h(x)
for all
x\in[x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}}]
, with strict inequality for some
x\in\{x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q}+1},\dotsc,x_{a_{q-1}}\}
, and we can take
\tilde{h}
to be any affine function such that
\tilde{h}>h
on
[x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}}]
.

•

I=(x_{a_{q}},\tilde{x})
for some
\tilde{x}\in(x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}})
: by continuity,
g(\tilde{x})=h(\tilde{x})
, and we must have
g(x_{a_{q-1}})>h(x_{a_{q-1}})
since
I\neq(x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}})
. Thus, we can take
\tilde{h}
to be any affine function satisfying
\tilde{h}(\tilde{x})=h(\tilde{x})
and
\tilde{h}(x_{a_{q}})>h(x_{a_{q}})
, so that
g\leq h\leq\tilde{h}
on
[x_{a_{q}},\tilde{x}]
,
g\geq h\geq\tilde{h}
on
[\tilde{x},x_{a_{q-1}}]
and
g(x_{a_{q-1}})>h(x_{a_{q-1}})>\tilde{h}(x_{a_{q-1}})
.

•

I=(\tilde{x},x_{a_{q-1}})
for some
\tilde{x}\in(x_{a_{q}},x_{a_{q-1}})
: similarly, we can take
\tilde{h}
to be any affine function satisfying
\tilde{h}(\tilde{x})=h(\tilde{x})
and
\tilde{h}(x_{a_{q-1}})>h(x_{a_{q-1}})
.

Now let
\lambda^{\prime}:=(h-\tilde{h})(x_{a_{q}})
and
\vartheta^{\prime}:=(h-\tilde{h})(x_{a_{q-1}})
. Then for each
i\in J_{q}
, we have
(h-\tilde{h})(x_{i})=(1-t_{i}^{J_{q}})\lambda^{\prime}+t_{i}^{J_{q}}\vartheta^{\prime}
since
h-\tilde{h}
is an affine function, and recall that
(g-h)(x_{i})=u_{i}^{\ell}-v_{i}^{J_{q}}(\lambda,\vartheta)
. Thus,
\lambda^{\prime},\vartheta^{\prime}
have the required properties.
∎

In conclusion, by the observation after (
S38
) and Claim
2
, we have

\sgn\bigl(\langle(I-P_{A})u^{\ell},e_{a_{s}}\rangle\bigr)=\sgn\bigl((u^{\ell}-\tilde{z}(\tilde{\lambda},\tilde{\vartheta}))_{a_{s}}\bigr)=\begin{cases}\sgn\bigl(\tilde{\lambda}\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)=\sgn\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{+}}\bigr)&\quad\text{if }q\leq s\leq m\\
\sgn\bigl(\tilde{\vartheta}\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{-}}\bigr)=\sgn\bigl(\tilde{v}_{a_{s}}^{J_{q}^{-}}\bigr)&\quad\text{if }0\leq s\leq q-1,\end{cases}

which together with Claim
1
implies (
S36
), as desired.
∎

Proof of Proposition
S18
.

For fixed
A\subseteq[j-1]
and
\ell\in[j-1]
,
let
\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}^{A}(u)=\lambda_{\ell}(P_{A}u)
and
\hat{\zeta}_{\ell}^{A}(u)=\langle u^{\ell},(I-P_{A})u\rangle
be as in (
19
), where
u
is some positive multiple of
e_{j}
. Enumerate the elements of
A
as
j=a_{0}>a_{1}>\cdots>a_{m}>a_{m+1}=0
and let
q^{\prime}:=\min(\{2\leq\tilde{q}\leq m:a_{\tilde{q}-1}=a_{\tilde{q}}+1\}\cup\{m+1\})
. Now
P_{A}u\in\mathcal{L}_{A}
, and if
j-1\notin A
, then for all
s\in\{0,\dotsc,m\}
, it follows by taking
\ell=j-1
and
q=1
in (
S36
) that

\langle P_{A}u,e_{a_{s}}\rangle\>\begin{cases}>0&\;\text{if }s<q^{\prime}\text{ and }s\text{ is odd}\\
<0&\;\text{if }s<q^{\prime}\text{ and }s\text{ is even}\\
=0&\;\text{if }s\geq q^{\prime}.\end{cases}

For
\ell\in[j-1]
, we deduce from this and (
16
) that

\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}^{A}(u)=\lambda_{\ell}(P_{A}u)\>\begin{cases}>0&\;\text{if }\ell=a_{s}\text{ for some odd }1\leq s\leq q^{\prime}\\
<0&\;\text{if }\ell=a_{s}\text{ for some even }1\leq s\leq q^{\prime}\\
=0&\;\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(S40)

Moreover, if
j-1\notin A
, then for
\ell\in[j-1]
, it follows by taking
s=0
in (
S36
) that

\hat{\zeta}_{\ell}^{A}(u)=\langle(I-P_{A})u^{\ell},u\rangle\>\begin{cases}>0&\;\text{if }a_{q}<\ell<a_{q-1}\text{ for some odd }q\in[q^{\prime}]\\
<0&\;\text{if }a_{q}<\ell<a_{q-1}\text{ for some even }q\in[q^{\prime}]\\
=0&\;\text{if }\ell\leq a_{q^{\prime}}=\ell_{r}\text{ or }\ell\in A.\end{cases}

(S41)

We are now in a position to show that under modification (IV’), Algorithm
7
cannot remain indefinitely at any of the thresholds
t_{r}
. To this end, it suffices to verify that if
r\in\mathbb{N}
is such that
t_{r}=t_{r+1}=t_{r+2}
, then
\ell_{\max}:=\max(A_{r}^{-}\cup A_{r}^{+})>\max(A_{r+1}^{-}\cup A_{r+1}^{+})
. First, we prove that
\ell_{\max}\notin A_{r+1}^{-}\cup A_{r+1}^{+}
. Enumerating the elements of
A\equiv A_{r}
as
a_{1}>\cdots>a_{m}
and defining
a_{0},q^{\prime}
as above, we consider separately the cases
\ell_{\max}\in A_{r}^{-}
and
\ell_{\max}\in A_{r}^{+}
.

•

If
\ell_{\max}\in A_{r}^{-}
, then
\beta_{\ell_{\max}}(t_{r+1})=0
and
\hat{\lambda}_{\ell_{\max}}^{A_{r}}(u)>0
. This means that
\ell_{\max}=a_{s}
for some odd
s\in[q^{\prime}]
. Indeed, when
j-1\notin A_{r}
, this follows from (
S40
), and otherwise if
j-1\in A_{r}
,
then
A_{r}^{-}=\{j-1\}
and
\ell_{\max}=j-1=a_{1}
. Now
A_{r+1}=A_{r}\setminus\{\ell_{\max}\}\subseteq[j-2]
under (IV’), so
\ell_{\max}\notin A_{r+1}^{-}\subseteq A_{r+1}
, and enumerating the elements of
A_{r+1}
as
a_{1}>\cdots>a_{s-1}>a_{s}^{\prime}>a_{s+1}^{\prime}>\cdots>a_{m-1}^{\prime}
, we have
a_{s}^{\prime}<\ell_{\max}<a_{s-1}
. Since
s\;(\leq q^{\prime})
is odd, we deduce from (
S41
) that
\hat{\zeta}_{\ell_{\max}}^{A_{r+1}}(u)>0
, and hence that
\ell_{\max}\notin A_{r+1}^{+}
.

•

Otherwise, if
\ell_{\max}\in A_{r}^{+}
, then
\gamma_{\ell_{\max}}(t_{r+1})=0
and
\hat{\zeta}_{\ell_{\max}}^{A_{r}}(u)<0
. In this case, we necessarily have
j-1\notin A_{r}
, since otherwise
A_{r}^{+}=\emptyset
, so it follows from (
S41
) that
a_{s}<\ell_{\max}<a_{s-1}
for some even
s\in[q^{\prime}-1]
. Now
A_{r+1}=A_{r}\cup\{\ell_{\max}\}\subseteq[j-2]
under (IV’), so
\ell_{\max}\notin A_{r+1}^{+}\subseteq A_{r+1}^{c}
, and
A_{r+1}
can be enumerated as
a_{1}>\cdots>a_{s-1}>a_{s}^{\prime}>a_{s+1}^{\prime}>\cdots>a_{m+1}^{\prime}
, where
a_{s}^{\prime}=\ell_{\max}
. Since
s
is even, we deduce from (
S40
) that
\hat{\lambda}_{\ell_{\max}}^{A_{r+1}}(u)\leq 0
,
and hence that
\ell_{\max}\notin A_{r+1}^{-}
.

It remains to show that
A_{r+1}^{-}\cup A_{r+1}^{+}
does not contain any
\ell\in\{\ell_{\max}+1,\dotsc,j-1\}
. If
\ell_{\max}=j-1
, then there is nothing to prove, so we assume that
\ell_{\max}<j-1
, in which case
j-1\notin A_{r}
by the arguments above. Writing
a_{q}^{\prime}
for the
q^{th}
largest element of
A_{r+1}
, we see that in both cases above,
a_{1}^{\prime}=a_{1}>\cdots>a_{s-1}^{\prime}=a_{s-1}
are precisely the indices in
A_{r+1}
that are strictly greater than
\ell_{\max}
, where
s\leq q^{\prime}
. Now fix
j-1\geq\ell>\ell_{\max}
and note that since
t_{r+1}=t_{r+2}
by assumption, we have the following:

•

Suppose that
\ell\in A_{r+1}
and
\beta_{\ell}(t_{r+2})=0
, in which case
\beta_{\ell}(t_{r+1})=0
,
\ell\in A_{r}
and
\ell\notin A_{r}^{-}
by the definition of
\ell_{\max}
. Thus,
\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}^{A_{r}}(u)\leq 0
, so by applying (
S40
) to
A_{r}
,
we deduce that
\ell\neq a_{q}
for any odd
q\in[q^{\prime}]
. Since
a_{q}=a_{q}^{\prime}
for
q\leq s-1
and
\ell>a_{q}^{\prime}
for
q\geq s
, this means that
\ell\neq a_{q}^{\prime}
for any odd
q
. Applying (
S40
) once again to
A_{r+1}
, we conclude that
\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}^{A_{r+1}}(u)\leq 0
, whence
\ell\notin A_{r+1}^{-}
.

•

Suppose that
\ell\notin A_{r+1}
and
\gamma_{\ell}(t_{r+2})=0
, in which case
\gamma_{\ell}(t_{r+1})=0
,
\ell\notin A_{r}
and
\ell\notin A_{r}^{+}
by the definition of
\ell_{\max}
. Thus,
\hat{\zeta}_{\ell}^{A_{r}}(u)\geq 0
, so in view of (
S41
), we cannot have
a_{q}<\ell<a_{q-1}
for any even
q\in[q^{\prime}-1]
. As above, it follows that we cannot have
a_{q}^{\prime}<\ell<a_{q-1}^{\prime}
for any even
q
. Applying (
S41
) once again to
A_{r+1}
, we conclude that
\hat{\zeta}_{\ell}^{A_{r+1}}(u)\geq 0
, whence
\ell\notin A_{r+1}^{+}
.

This completes the justification of (IV’). Finally, we obtain both assertions of Proposition
S18
as straightforward consequences of (
S40
) and (
S41
).

(a) By taking
q=1
in the first line of (
S41
), we see that
\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}^{A_{r}}(u)>0
for all
\max A_{r}=a_{1}<\ell\leq j-1
. Thus, in Algorithm
7
with modification (IV’),
A_{r+1}\subseteq A_{r}\cup A_{r}^{+}\subseteq\{1,\dotsc,\max A_{r}\}
.

(b) Since
\ell_{r}=a_{q^{\prime}}
here, this follows immediately from the final lines of (
S40
) and (
S41
).
∎

S5
Auxiliary results for Section
S2

S5.1
Auxiliary results for Section
S2.1

The proof of Theorem
5
relies on the following bound on the localised Gaussian widths of the cone
\Gamma[\mathcal{D}]=\bigl\{\bigl(f(x_{1}),\dotsc,f(x_{n})\bigr):f\in\mathcal{F}\bigr\}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
\mathcal{D}
is a set of design points
x_{1}<\cdots<x_{n}
in
[0,1]
and
\mathcal{F}
is the class of all S-shaped functions on
[0,1]
. For
\theta\equiv(\theta_{1},\dotsc,\theta_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
r>0
, recall from Section
S2
that we defined
V(\theta)=\theta_{n}-\theta_{1}
and
\Gamma(\theta,r)\equiv\Gamma(\theta,r)[\mathcal{D}]=\{v\in\Gamma[\mathcal{D}]:\|v-\theta\|\leq r\}
.

Lemma S21
.

For a set
\mathcal{D}\subseteq[0,1]
of design points
x_{1}<\cdots<x_{n}
with
n\geq 2
, define
\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})
as in (
S10
). Fix
\theta\in\Gamma[\mathcal{D}]
and
r>0
. If
Z\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
, then for all
\tilde{C}\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,\biggl(\sup_{v\in\Gamma(\theta,r)}\>\lvert Z^{\top}(v-\theta)\rvert\biggr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{r^{2}}{\tilde{C}}+r\sqrt{\log n}+\bigl(V(\theta)+\tilde{C}\bigr)^{1/4}\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})^{1/8}\,r^{3/4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{r^{2}}{\tilde{C}}+r\sqrt{\log n}+\bigl(V(\theta)+\tilde{C}\bigr)^{1/4}\biggl(\frac{x_{n}-x_{1}}{\min_{2\leq i\leq n}(x_{i}-x_{i-1})}\biggr)^{1/8}\,r^{3/4}.

(S42)

We will derive this result from the bounds in Lemma
S22
and
S23
on the covering numbers of

\displaystyle\Gamma_{A,B}[\mathcal{D}]

\displaystyle:=\{(v_{1},\dotsc,v_{n})\in\Gamma[\mathcal{D}]:A\leq v_{i}\leq B\text{ for all }i\}

\displaystyle K_{A,B}[\mathcal{D}]

\displaystyle:=\bigl\{\bigl(f(x_{1}),\dotsc,f(x_{n})\bigr),\,f\text{ is convex },\,A\leq f\leq B\bigr\},

where
-\infty<A<B<\infty
. For
\varepsilon>0
and
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, recall that
\mathcal{N}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
is said to be an
\varepsilon
-cover
of
U
(with respect to the Euclidean norm
\|{\cdot}\|
) if
U\subseteq\bigcup_{u\in\mathcal{N}}\bar{B}(u,\varepsilon)
, where
\bar{B}(u,\varepsilon):=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\|v-u\|\leq\varepsilon\}
. We denote by
N(\varepsilon,U):=\inf\{\lvert\mathcal{N}\rvert:\mathcal{N}\text{ is an }\varepsilon\text{-cover of }U\}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
the
\varepsilon
-covering number
of
U
.

Lemma S22
.

In the setting of Lemma
S21
, the following holds for all
-\infty<A<B<\infty
and
\varepsilon>0
:

\log N(\varepsilon,\Gamma_{A,B}[\mathcal{D}])\lesssim\log n+(B-A)^{1/2}\,\frac{\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})^{1/4}}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}.

(S43)

Lemma S23
.

For any finite set
\mathcal{D}\subseteq[0,1]
and every
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\log N(\varepsilon,K_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}])\lesssim\frac{\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})^{1/4}}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}.

(S44)

We first give the proof of (
S44
), which gives rise to the definition of
\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})
in (
S10
), and then deduce Lemmas
S22
and
S21
in that order.

Proof of Lemma
S23
.

We proceed by induction on
n=\lvert\mathcal{D}\rvert
: for
n=1
, the bound clearly holds since
\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})=1
by definition, so suppose now that
\mathcal{D}=\{x_{1}<\cdots<x_{n}\}
for some
n\geq 2
. First, by taking
c_{1}=n\min_{2\leq i\leq n}(x_{i}-x_{i-1})
in the second bound in
Guntuboyina and Sen 2013
and then arguing as in the proof of
Chatterjee 2016

Chatterjee 2016
, we see that

\log N(\varepsilon,K_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}])\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}\biggl(\frac{(x_{n}-x_{1})}{\min_{2\leq i\leq n}(x_{i}-x_{i-1})}\biggr)^{1/4}.

(S45)

In addition, for a fixed partition of
\mathcal{D}
into
k\geq 2
non-empty sets
\mathcal{D}_{1},\dotsc,\mathcal{D}_{k}
, we now define
\tilde{a}_{\ell}:=\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D}_{\ell})^{1/10}/\bigl(\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k}\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D}_{\ell^{\prime}})^{1/5}\bigr)^{1/2}
for
\ell\in[k]
. Given
\varepsilon>0
, let
\varepsilon_{\ell}:=\varepsilon\tilde{a}_{\ell}
for each
\ell
, so that
\varepsilon^{2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\varepsilon_{\ell}^{2}
. Then since
\lvert\mathcal{D}_{1}\rvert,\dotsc,\lvert\mathcal{D}_{k}\rvert<\lvert\mathcal{D}\rvert
, it follows by induction that

\log N(\varepsilon,K_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}])\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\log N(\varepsilon_{\ell},K_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}_{\ell}])\lesssim\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\,\frac{\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D}_{\ell})^{1/4}}{\varepsilon_{\ell}^{1/2}}=\frac{\bigl(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D}_{\ell})^{1/5}\bigr)^{5/4}}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},

(S46)

where by Lemma
S24
below (with
b_{\ell}=\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D}_{\ell})^{1/2}
for all
\ell
), our choice of
\varepsilon_{1},\dotsc,\varepsilon_{k}
minimises the penultimate expression above subject to the constraint
\varepsilon^{2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\varepsilon_{\ell}^{2}
. Minimising the right hand side of (
S46
) over all partitions of
\mathcal{D}
into
k\geq 2
non-empty subsets, we can combine (
S45
) and (
S46
) to complete the inductive step for (
S44
), in view of the definition of
\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})
in (
S10
).
∎

Lemma S24
.

For fixed
b_{1},\dotsc,b_{k}>0
, the unique solution to the optimisation problem

\min\;\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\left(\frac{b_{\ell}}{a_{\ell}}\right)^{1/2}\quad\text{subject to }\;\;\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}a_{\ell}^{2}=1,\,a_{\ell}>0\text{ for }\ell\in[k]

is given by
\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}(b_{\ell}/a_{\ell}^{*})^{1/2}=\bigl(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}b_{\ell}^{2/5}\bigr)^{5/4}
, where
a_{\ell}^{*}:=b_{\ell}^{1/5}/\bigl(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}b_{\ell}^{2/5}\bigr)^{1/2}
for each
\ell
.

Proof of Lemma
S24
.

Let
\tau=2/5
,
p=2/(2-\tau)
and
q=2/\tau
, so that
1/p+1/q=1
,
\tau p=1/2
and
\tau q=2
. Then by Hölder’s inequality,

\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}b_{\ell}^{2/5}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}b_{\ell}^{\tau}\leq\left\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\left(\frac{b_{\ell}^{\tau}}{a_{\ell}^{\tau}}\right)^{p}\right\}^{1/p}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}a_{\ell}^{\tau q}\right)^{1/q}=\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}b_{\ell}^{2/5}\right)^{5/4},

with equality if and only if
a_{\ell}=b_{\ell}^{p/(p+q)}/\lambda=b_{\ell}^{1/5}/\lambda
for all
\ell
, where taking
\lambda=\bigl(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}b_{\ell}^{2/5}\bigr)^{1/2}
ensures that
\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}a_{\ell}^{2}=1
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
S22
.

By a scaling argument, it suffices to show that

\log N(\varepsilon,\Gamma_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}])\lesssim\log n+\frac{\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})^{1/4}}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}

(S47)

for all
\varepsilon>0
, i.e. that (
S43
) holds when
A=0
and
B=1
. Indeed, for general
-\infty<A<B<\infty
, define the invertible affine map
L_{A,B}\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
by
L_{A,B}(v)_{i}:=A+(B-A)v_{i}
for
v\equiv(v_{1},\dotsc,v_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
i\in[n]
, so that
\Gamma_{A,B}[\mathcal{D}]=\{L_{A,B}(v):v\in\Gamma_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}]\}
. If (
S47
) holds, then for any
\varepsilon>0
, we can find an
\varepsilon/(B-A)
-cover
\mathcal{N}
of
\Gamma_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}]
with
\log\,\lvert\mathcal{N}\rvert\lesssim\log n+(B-A)^{1/2}\,(\sqrt{Rn}/\varepsilon)^{1/2}
. For any
\theta\in\Gamma_{A,B}[\mathcal{D}]
, there exists
\theta^{*}\in\mathcal{N}
satisfying
\|\theta-L_{A,B}(\theta^{*})\|=(B-A)\|L_{A,B}^{-1}(\theta)-\theta^{*}\|\leq\varepsilon
, so
\mathcal{N}_{A,B}:=\{L_{A,B}(v):v\in\mathcal{N}\}
is an
\varepsilon
-cover of
\Gamma_{A,B}[\mathcal{D}]
with
\log\,\lvert\mathcal{N}_{A,B}\rvert=\log\,\lvert\mathcal{N}\rvert\lesssim\log n+(B-A)^{1/2}\,(\sqrt{Rn}/\varepsilon)^{1/2}
, as desired.

To establish (
S47
), fix
\varepsilon>0
and let
\Gamma_{0,1}^{m}[\mathcal{D}]:=\bigl\{\bigl(f(x_{1}),\dotsc,f(x_{n})\bigr):f\in\mathcal{F}^{m},\,0\leq f\leq 1\bigr\}
for
m\in[0,1]
, so that
\Gamma_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}]=\bigcup_{j=1}^{\,n}\Gamma_{0,1}^{x_{j}}[\mathcal{D}]
and

N(\varepsilon,\Gamma_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}])\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}N(\varepsilon,\Gamma_{0,1}^{x_{j}}[\mathcal{D}])\leq n\max_{1\leq j\leq n}N(\varepsilon,\Gamma_{0,1}^{x_{j}}[\mathcal{D}]).

Now for
j\in[n]
, let
\mathcal{D}_{j}^{-}:=\{x_{i}:1\leq i\leq j\}
and
\mathcal{D}_{j}^{+}:=\{x_{i}:j+1\leq i\leq n\}
. Then
\Gamma_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}]\subseteq K_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}_{j}^{-}]\times\bigl(-K_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}_{j}^{+}]\bigr)
and
\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D}_{j}^{\pm})\leq\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})
by (
S10
), so it follows from Lemma
S23
that

\log N(\varepsilon,\Gamma_{0,1}^{x_{j}}[\mathcal{D}])\leq\log N\bigl(\varepsilon/\sqrt{2},K_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}_{j}^{-}]\bigr)+\log N\bigl(\varepsilon/\sqrt{2},K_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}_{j}^{+}]\bigr)\lesssim\frac{\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})^{1/4}}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}.

We conclude that

\log N(\varepsilon,\Gamma_{0,1}[\mathcal{D}])\leq\log n+\max_{1\leq j\leq n}\log N(\varepsilon_{\ell},\Gamma_{0,1}^{x_{j}}[\mathcal{D}])\lesssim\log n+\frac{\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})^{1/4}}{\varepsilon^{1/2}},

as required.
∎

When
\varepsilon\gg B-A
, it turns out that in the proof above, we do not have to construct separate
\varepsilon
-covers for each of the sets
\Gamma_{A,B}^{x_{1}}[\mathcal{D}],\dotsc,\Gamma_{A,B}^{x_{n}}[\mathcal{D}]
individually. This is because elements of
\Gamma_{A,B}^{x_{j}}[\mathcal{D}]
can be approximated to accuracy
\varepsilon
by those in a covering set for
\Gamma_{A,B}^{x_{j^{\prime}}}[\mathcal{D}]
with
j^{\prime}
close to
j
. In general, we can improve the first
\log n
term in (
S43
) to
\log\bigl(1\vee\{n(B-A)^{2}/\varepsilon^{2}\}\wedge n\bigr)
, and hence obtain an overall bound in Lemma
S22
that tends to 0 as
\varepsilon\rightarrow\infty
. We omit further details of these additional arguments, since this improved result leads to the same worst-case oracle inequality (
9
) as in Theorem
5
(possibly with a slightly smaller universal constant
C
).

Proof of Lemma
S21
.

Fix
\theta\in\Gamma
and let
Z\sim N_{n}(0,I_{n})
. For every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
A_{k}:=\theta_{1}-2^{k}=\min_{1\leq i\leq n}\theta_{i}-2^{k}
and
B_{k}:=\theta_{n}+2^{k}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\theta_{i}+2^{k}
, and define
\pi_{k}(s):=s\vee A_{k}\wedge B_{k}
for
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Note that if
v\in\Gamma
, then
\pi_{k}(v):=(\pi_{k}(v_{1}),\dotsc,\pi_{k}(v_{n}))\in\Gamma_{A_{k},B_{k}}=:\tilde{\Gamma}_{k}
. Moreover,
\theta\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{k}
in view of our choice of
A_{k},B_{k}
, and if
v\in\Gamma(\theta,r)
for some
r>0
, then
\pi_{k}(v)\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{k}(\theta,r)
. Consequently, for any
r>0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,\biggl(\sup_{v\in\Gamma(\theta,r)}\>\lvert Z^{\top}(v-\theta)\rvert\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\,\biggl(\sup_{v\in\Gamma(\theta,r)}\>\bigl|Z^{\top}\bigl(\pi_{k}(v)-\theta\bigr)\bigr|\biggr)+\mathbb{E}\,\biggl(\sup_{v\in\Gamma(\theta,r)}\>\bigl|Z^{\top}\bigl(v-\pi_{k}(v)\bigr)\bigr|\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\,\biggl(\sup_{u\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{k}(\theta,r)}\>\lvert Z^{\top}(u-\theta)\rvert\biggr)+\mathbb{E}\,\biggl(\sup_{v\in\Gamma(\theta,r)}\>\bigl|Z^{\top}\bigl(v-\pi_{k}(v)\bigr)\bigr|\biggr).

(S48)

To bound the first term in (
S48
), observe first that by the triangle inequality,
\tilde{\Gamma}_{k}(\theta,r)
has diameter
d:=\sup\{\|v-v^{\prime}\|:v,v^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{k}(\theta,r)\}\leq 2r
. We can now apply Lemma
S22
in conjunction with Dudley’s metric entropy bound for Gaussian processes
(e.g.
Giné and Nickl 2016
, Theorem 2.3.7)
to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,\biggl(\sup_{u\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{k}(\theta,r)}\>\lvert Z^{\top}(u-\theta)\rvert\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{d/2}\sqrt{\log 2N\bigl(\varepsilon,\tilde{\Gamma}_{k}(\theta,r)\bigr)}\,d\varepsilon\leq 4\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{r}\sqrt{\log 2N(\varepsilon,\Gamma_{A_{k},B_{k}})}\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{r}\,\bigl\{\sqrt{\log n}+(B_{k}-A_{k})^{1/4}\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})^{1/8}\,\varepsilon^{-1/4}\bigr\}\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim r\sqrt{\log n}+\bigl(V(\theta)+2^{k}\bigr)^{1/4}\tilde{R}(\mathcal{D})^{1/8}\,r^{3/4}.

(S49)

As for the second term in (
S48
), we define
I_{1,\ell}(v):=\{1\leq i\leq n:A_{\ell+1}<v_{i}\leq A_{\ell}\}
and
I_{2,\ell}(v):=\{1\leq i\leq n:B_{\ell}\leq v_{i}<B_{\ell+1}\}
for
v\equiv(v_{1},\dotsc,v_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that if
j\in I_{1,\ell}(v)
for some
\ell\geq k
, then
\theta_{j}-v_{j}\geq\theta_{1}-A_{\ell}=2^{\ell}
and
0\leq\pi_{k}(v_{j})-v_{j}<\theta_{1}-A_{\ell+1}=2^{\ell+1}
. Similarly,
v_{j}-\theta_{j}\geq 2^{\ell}
and
0\leq v_{j}-\pi_{k}(v_{j})<2^{\ell+1}
for all
j\in I_{2,\ell}(v)
. Thus, if
v\in\Gamma(\theta,r)
, then
\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\theta_{i}-v_{i})^{2}\leq r^{2}
, so
\lvert I_{1,\ell}(v)\rvert\vee\lvert I_{2,\ell}(v)\rvert\leq r^{2}/2^{2\ell}
; in fact, since
v_{1}\leq\cdots\leq v_{n}
, this means that
I_{1,\ell}(v)\subseteq\{1,\dotsc,\lfloor r^{2}/2^{2\ell}\rfloor\}
and
I_{2,\ell}(v)\subseteq\{n+1-i:1\leq i\leq\lfloor r^{2}/2^{2\ell}\rfloor\}
. Consequently, for every
v\in\Gamma(\theta,r)
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|Z^{\top}\bigl(v-\pi_{k}(v)\bigr)\bigr|\leq\sum_{\ell=k}^{\infty}\,\sum_{i\in I_{1,\ell}(v)\,\cup\,I_{2,\ell}(v)}\lvert Z_{i}\rvert\,\lvert v_{i}-\pi_{k}(v_{i})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=k}^{\infty}\,2^{\ell+1}\sum_{i\in I_{1,\ell}(v)\,\cup\,I_{2,\ell}(v)}\lvert Z_{i}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=k}^{\infty}\,2^{\ell+1}\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor r^{2}/2^{2\ell}\rfloor}(\lvert Z_{i}\rvert+\lvert Z_{n+1-i}\rvert),

so

\mathbb{E}\,\biggl(\sup_{v\in\Gamma(\theta,r)}\>\bigl|Z^{\top}\bigl(v-\pi_{k}(v)\bigr)\bigr|\biggr)\leq\sum_{\ell=k}^{\infty}\,2^{\ell+2}\sum_{i=1}^{\lfloor r^{2}/2^{2\ell}\rfloor}\mathbb{E}(\lvert Z_{i}\rvert)\lesssim\sum_{\ell=k}^{\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{2^{\ell}}\lesssim\frac{r^{2}}{2^{k}}.

(S50)

Finally, for any
\tilde{C}\geq 1
, let
k\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
2^{k-1}\leq\tilde{C}<2^{k}
. The desired bound (
S21
) then follows from (
S48
), (
S49
) and (
S50
).
∎

S5.2
Auxiliary results for Section
S2.2

Here, we establish the key technical Lemmas
S5
–
S7
that form part of the proof of Theorem
7
, as well as Lemma
S8
from the proof of Proposition
8
.
Lemma
S25
below is the starting point for the proof of Lemma
S5
, and applies to general configurations of design points
x_{1}<\cdots<x_{n}
(which need not be equispaced).

Lemma S25
.

Let
x_{k}
be a kink of the convex LSE
\hat{g}_{n}
based on
(x_{1},Y_{1}),\dotsc,(x_{n},Y_{n})
. Let
\bar{x}_{L}:=k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}x_{i}
and
\bar{x}:=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}
. Then

\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{L})Y_{i}}{\sum_{i=1}^{k}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{L})^{2}}\leq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(x_{i}-\bar{x})Y_{i}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(x_{i}-\bar{x})^{2}}.

In other words, the slope of the regression line fitted using
\{(x_{i},Y_{i}):1\leq i\leq k\}
is at most that of the regression line fitted using
\{(x_{i},Y_{i}):1\leq i\leq n\}
.

Proof.

Let
S_{L}^{2}:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{L})^{2}
and
S^{2}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(x_{i}-\bar{x})^{2}
. Then

S^{2}\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}(x_{i}-\bar{x})^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{L})^{2}+k(\bar{x}_{L}-\bar{x})^{2}\geq S_{L}^{2}>0,

since
k\geq 2
. The linear functions
h_{L},h_{R}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
defined by
h_{L}(x):=S_{L}^{-2}(x-\bar{x}_{L})-S^{-2}(x-\bar{x})
and
h_{R}(x):=-S^{-2}(x-\bar{x})
have slopes
S_{L}^{-2}-S^{-2}\geq 0
and
-S^{-2}<0
respectively. Now let
h\in\mathcal{G}
be such that
h(x_{i})=h_{L}(x_{i})
for
i\in[k]
and
h(x_{i})=h_{R}(x_{i})
for
k+1\leq i\leq n
. Since
h_{L}(x_{k})=S_{L}^{-2}(x_{k}-\bar{x}_{L})-S^{-2}(x_{k}-\bar{x})\geq-S^{-2}(x_{k}-\bar{x})=h_{R}(x_{k})
, this means that
h
is convex on both
[x_{1},x_{k}]
and
[x_{k},x_{n}]
(and locally concave at
x_{k}
, a kink of
\hat{g}_{n}
). Therefore,
\hat{g}_{n}+\eta h\in\mathcal{G}
is convex for sufficiently small
\eta>0
, whence
\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(x_{i})\bigl(Y_{i}-\hat{g}_{n}(x_{i})\bigr)\leq 0
by (
S3
) or Lemma
S17
.

To establish that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(x_{i})\,Y_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}(h_{L}-h_{R})(x_{i})\,Y_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{R}(x_{i})\,Y_{i}\leq 0
, as claimed in the lemma, it therefore suffices to show that

\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(x_{i})\,\hat{g}_{n}(x_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{k}(h_{L}-h_{R})(x_{i})\,\hat{g}_{n}(x_{i})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{R}(x_{i})\,\hat{g}_{n}(x_{i})\leq 0,

i.e. that the slope of the regression line fitted using
\{(x_{i},\hat{g}_{n}(x_{i})):1\leq i\leq k\}
is at most that of the regression line fitted using
\{(x_{i},\hat{g}_{n}(x_{i})):1\leq i\leq n\}
. To this end, for
j\in[n]
, let
K^{1,j}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{j}
be the closed, convex cone of convex sequences based on
x_{1},\dotsc,x_{j}
, as defined at the start of Section
S2
, and define
\hat{v}^{j}:=\bigl(\hat{g}_{n}(x_{1}),\dotsc,\hat{g}_{n}(x_{j})\bigr)\in K^{1,j}
. Let
\pm u^{j,0},\pm u^{j,1},u^{j,2},\dotsc,u^{j,j-1}\in K^{1,j}
be its generators, where
u_{i}^{j,0}=1
and
u_{i}^{j,\ell}=(x_{i}-x_{\ell})^{+}
for all
i\in[j]
and
\ell\in[j-1]
as in the paragraph containing (
6
). Since
\hat{v}^{n}\in K^{1,n}
, we can write
\hat{v}^{n}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}u^{\ell}
for some
\hat{\lambda}_{0},\dotsc,\hat{\lambda}_{n-1}\in\mathbb{R}
with
\hat{\lambda}_{2},\dotsc,\hat{\lambda}_{n-1}\geq 0
. Let
A_{L}:=\{0,1\}\cup\{2\leq\ell\leq k-1:\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}>0\}
and
A_{R}:=\{k\leq\ell\leq n-1:\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}>0\}
, so that

\hat{v}^{k}=\sum_{\ell\in A_{L}}\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}u^{k,\ell}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{v}^{n}=\sum_{\ell\in A_{L}}\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}u^{n,\ell}+\sum_{\ell\in A_{R}}\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}u^{n,\ell}.

For
j\in[n]
, let
\tilde{P}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{j\times j}
represent the orthogonal projection onto
L_{j}:=\Span\{u^{j,0},u^{j,1}\}
,
so that if
z\in\mathbb{R}^{j}
, then
\tilde{P}_{j}z
is the vector of fitted values from ordinary least squares regression based on
\{(x_{i},z_{i}):1\leq i\leq j\}
. We say that
v\in L_{j}
has
slope

b
if
v_{i}-v_{i-1}=b(x_{i}-x_{i-1})
for
2\leq i\leq j
, and denote by
b_{j\ell}
the slope of
\tilde{P}_{j}u^{j,\ell}
for
0\leq\ell\leq j-1
. Since
k\leq n
, observe that
0\leq b_{k\ell}\leq b_{n\ell}\leq 1
for all
0\leq\ell\leq k-1
and
b_{k\ell}=b_{n\ell}
for
r\in\{0,1\}
. Writing
b_{k}
and
b_{n}
for the slopes of
\tilde{P}_{k}\hat{v}^{k}=\sum_{\ell\in A_{L}}\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}\tilde{P}_{k}u^{k,\ell}
and
\tilde{P}_{n}\hat{v}^{n}=\sum_{\ell\in A_{L}}\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}\tilde{P}_{n}u^{n,\ell}+\sum_{\ell\in A_{R}}\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}\tilde{P}_{n}u^{n,\ell}
respectively, we conclude that

b_{k}=\sum_{\ell\in A_{L}}\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}\,b_{k\ell}\leq\sum_{\ell\in A_{L}}\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}\,b_{n\ell}+\sum_{\ell\in A_{R}}\hat{\lambda}_{\ell}\,b_{n\ell}=b_{n}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
S5
.

If
\hat{\tau}_{0L}\neq m_{0}
(i.e.
\{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}\}
is non-empty), then
\hat{\tau}_{0L}
is a kink of
\hat{g}_{n,0}
in
(m_{0},(m_{0}+\tilde{m}_{+})/2]
. Define
N_{01}:=\lvert\{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}\}\rvert\vee 1
,
N_{0}:=\lvert\{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}\}\rvert
,
\bar{x}_{01}:=N_{01}^{-1}\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}x_{i}
and
\bar{x}_{0}:=N_{0}^{-1}\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}x_{i}
, where we suppress the dependence on
n
for convenience. We deduce from Lemma
S25
that if
N_{01}\geq 2
, then

\frac{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})Y_{i}}{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})^{2}}\leq\frac{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{0})Y_{i}}{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{0})^{2}},

and hence that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})f_{0}(x_{i})}{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})^{2}}-\frac{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{0})f_{0}(x_{i})}{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{0})^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq\frac{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{0})\,\xi_{i}}{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{0})^{2}}-\frac{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})\,\xi_{i}}{\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})^{2}}.

(S51)

Let
\beta_{L1},\beta_{L2}
be equal to the first and second terms respectively on the right-hand side of (
S51
) when
N_{01}\geq 2
(and set
\beta_{L1}=\beta_{L2}=0
otherwise). Taking into account the randomness of the intervals
\mathcal{I}_{01},\mathcal{I}_{0}
, we claim that
(\beta_{L1}-\beta_{L2})\sqrt{n(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})^{3}}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log n})
. Indeed, for fixed
1\leq a<b\leq n
, define
\bar{x}_{a:b}:=(b-a+1)^{-1}\sum_{i=a}^{b}x_{i}
,
S_{ab}^{2}:=\sum_{i=a}^{b}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{a:b})^{2}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{ab}:=S_{ab}^{-2}\,\sum_{i=a}^{b}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{a:b})\,\xi_{i}
. Under Assumption
2
, the design points
x_{i}\equiv x_{ni}=i/n
are equispaced and the errors
\xi_{i}
are sub-Gaussian with parameter 1, so
S_{ab}^{2}\asymp(b-a)^{3}/n^{2}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{ab}
has sub-Gaussian parameter
S_{ab}^{-2}\asymp n^{2}/(b-a)^{3}=n^{-1}(x_{b}-x_{a})^{-3}
. Therefore,
\tilde{\beta}_{\mathrm{max}}:=\max_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\,\lvert\tilde{\beta}_{ab}\rvert\sqrt{n(x_{b}-x_{a})^{3}}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log n})

(e.g.
Giné and Nickl 2016
, Lemma 2.3.4)
, so

\displaystyle\sqrt{n(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})^{3}}\,\lvert\beta_{L1}\rvert\leq 2^{3/2}\,\tilde{\beta}_{\mathrm{max}}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log n})

\displaystyle\sqrt{n(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})^{3}}\,\lvert\beta_{L2}\rvert\leq\sqrt{n(\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0})^{3}}\,\lvert\beta_{L2}\rvert\leq 2^{3/2}\,\tilde{\beta}_{\mathrm{max}}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log n}),

(S52)

which justifies the claim above. Now let
b_{L1},b_{L2}
be equal to the first and second terms respectively on the left-hand side of (
S51
) when
N_{01}\geq 2
(and set
b_{L1}=b_{L2}=0
otherwise). For
\gamma>1
and
x_{a}\in(m_{0},1]
, let
s_{\gamma}(x_{a}):=n^{-1}\sum_{i:x_{i}\in(m_{0},x_{a}]}(x_{i}-m_{0})^{\gamma}
, and observe that if
x_{j-1}\leq m_{0}<x_{j}<x_{a}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{(x_{a}-m_{0})^{\gamma+1}}{\gamma+1}\leq\int_{x_{j-1}}^{x_{a}}(x-m_{0})^{\gamma}\,dx\leq s_{\gamma}(x_{a})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{x_{j}}^{x_{a+1}}(x_{a}\wedge x-m_{0})^{\gamma}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(x_{a}-m_{0})^{\gamma+1}}{\gamma+1}\left(1+\frac{\gamma+1}{n(x_{a}-m_{0})}\right).

(S53)

We claim that if
\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0}\geq 2n^{-1/(2\alpha+1)}
, then

\displaystyle b_{L1}

\displaystyle=f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})-B\bigl(1+o_{p}(1)\bigr)\,\frac{s_{\alpha+1}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})-2^{-1}s_{\alpha}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})}{s_{2}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})-2^{-1}s_{1}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})}

\displaystyle\geq f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})-\frac{6\alpha B}{(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}\bigl(1+o_{p}(1)\bigr)(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})^{\alpha-1}

(S54)

and

\displaystyle b_{L2}

\displaystyle=f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})-B\bigl(1+o_{p}(1)\bigr)\,\frac{s_{\alpha+1}(\tilde{m}_{+})-2^{-1}s_{\alpha}(\tilde{m}_{+})(\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0})}{s_{2}(\tilde{m}_{+})-2^{-1}s_{1}(\tilde{m}_{+})(\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})-\frac{6\alpha B}{(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}\bigl(1+o_{p}(1)\bigr)(\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0})^{\alpha-1}

\displaystyle\leq f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})-2^{\alpha-1}\frac{6\alpha B}{(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}\bigl(1+o_{p}(1)\bigr)(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})^{\alpha-1}.

(S55)

To see this, recall that under (
13
) in Assumption
2
, we can write
f_{0}(x)=f_{0}(m_{0})+f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})(x-m_{0})-B\bigl(1+\eta(x-m_{0})\bigr)\sgn(x-m_{0})\lvert x-m_{0}\rvert^{\alpha}
for
x\in[0,1]
when
\alpha>1
, where
\eta(x-m_{0})\to 0
as
x\to m_{0}
. Writing
x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01}=(x_{i}-m_{0})-2^{-1}(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})
, we see that

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})\bigl(f_{0}(m_{0})+f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})(x_{i}-m_{0})\bigr)

\displaystyle\textstyle=f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})(x_{i}-m_{0})

\displaystyle\textstyle=f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})\bigl(s_{2}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})-2^{-1}s_{1}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})\bigr)

(S56)

\displaystyle\textstyle=f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})^{2}

\displaystyle\text{and}\qquad\textstyle\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})(x_{i}-m_{0})^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=s_{\alpha+1}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})-2^{-1}s_{\alpha}(\hat{\tau}_{0L}).

(S57)

Moreover, since
\omega(\delta):=\sup\,\{\lvert\eta(x-m_{0})\rvert:x\in[0,1],\,\lvert x-m_{0}\rvert\leq\delta\}\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
and
\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0}=o_{p}(1)
by Proposition
2
(a),
we have

\displaystyle\textstyle\lvert\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01})\,\eta(x_{i}-m_{0})(x_{i}-m_{0})^{\alpha}\rvert

\displaystyle\textstyle\leq\omega(\lvert\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0}\rvert)\sum_{i:x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{01}}\lvert x_{i}-\bar{x}_{01}\rvert(x_{i}-m_{0})^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\textstyle=o_{p}(1)\,\bigl(s_{\alpha+1}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})+2^{-1}s_{\alpha}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})\bigr).

(S58)

Combining (
S56
), (
S57
) and (
S58
), we obtain the first equality in (
S54
). On the event
\{\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0}\geq 2n^{-1/(2\alpha+1)}\}
, we find using (
S53
) that

\displaystyle\frac{s_{\alpha+1}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})-2^{-1}s_{\alpha}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})}{s_{2}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})-2^{-1}s_{1}(\hat{\tau}_{0L})(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\frac{\alpha}{2(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}+\frac{1}{n(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})}}{\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{2n(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})}}(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})^{\alpha-1}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\frac{6\alpha}{(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})^{\alpha-1},

which justifies the lower bound on
b_{L1}
in (
S54
). We can derive (
S55
) similarly by first establishing analogues of (
S56
), (
S57
) and (
S58
), and then applying (
S53
) to see that

\displaystyle\frac{s_{\alpha+1}(\tilde{m}_{+})-2^{-1}s_{\alpha}(\tilde{m}_{+})(\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0})}{s_{2}(\tilde{m}_{+})-2^{-1}s_{1}(\tilde{m}_{+})(\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0})}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\frac{\alpha}{2(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}-\frac{1}{2n(\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0})}}{\frac{1}{12}+\frac{1}{n(\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0})}}(\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0})^{\alpha-1}

\displaystyle\geq\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\frac{6\alpha}{(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}(\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0})^{\alpha-1}

on the event
E_{n}^{+}\supseteq\{\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0}\geq 2n^{-1/(2\alpha+1)}\}
. Since
\tilde{m}_{+}-m_{0}\geq 2(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})
and
\alpha>1
, this yields the upper bound on
b_{L2}
in (
S55
).

Thus, on the event
\{\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0}\geq 2n^{-1/(2\alpha+1)}\}
, we can apply (
S54
), (
S55
), (
S51
) and (
S52
) in that order to deduce that

\displaystyle(2^{\alpha-1}-1)\frac{6\alpha B}{(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}\bigl(1+o_{p}(1)\bigr)\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(b_{L1}-b_{L2})\sqrt{n(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})^{3}}

(S59)

\displaystyle\leq(\beta_{L1}-\beta_{L2})\sqrt{n(\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0})^{3}}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log n}).

We conclude that
\hat{\tau}_{0L}-m_{0}=O_{p}\bigl((n/\log n)^{-1/(2\alpha+1)}\bigr)
, as required.
∎

Proof of Lemma
S6
.

Since
C_{n}\to\infty
, we have

t_{n}=\sqrt{C_{n}}\,(n/\log n)^{-1/(2\alpha+1)}<4^{-1}C_{n}(n/\log n)^{-1/(2\alpha+1)}=u_{n}/2

for all sufficiently large
n
. For each
n
, let
a_{n}:=\lceil n(m_{0}+u_{n}/2)\rceil
and
b_{n}:=\lfloor n(m_{0}+u_{n})\rfloor
, so that
x_{a_{n}}<m_{0}+u_{n}/2\leq x_{a_{n}+1}
and
x_{b_{n}-1}\leq m_{0}+u_{n}<x_{b_{n}}
. Then for all sufficiently large
n
, we have
\inf_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{T}_{n}}\inf_{c_{0},c_{1}}\|\theta^{a,b}-c_{0}\mathbf{1}^{a,b}-c_{1}x^{a,b}\|^{2}\geq\inf_{c_{0},c_{1}}\|\theta^{a_{n},b_{n}}-c_{0}\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}}-c_{1}x^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}=:R_{n}
.

For
x\in[m_{0},1]
, recall from (
13
) in Assumption
2
that

f_{0}(x)=\begin{cases}f_{0}(m_{0})+f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})(x-m_{0})-B\bigl(1+\eta(x-m_{0})\bigr)(x-m_{0})^{\alpha}\quad&\text{when }\alpha>1\\
f_{0}(m_{0})+B\bigl(1+\eta(x-m_{0})\bigr)(x-m_{0})^{\alpha}\quad&\text{when }\alpha\in(0,1),\end{cases}

where
\eta(x-m_{0})\to 0
as
x\to m_{0}
. For each
n
, let
\widetilde{\theta}_{i}^{a_{n},b_{n}}:=B\bigl(1+\eta(x_{i}-m_{0})\bigr)(x_{i}-m_{0})^{\alpha}

for
m_{0}\leq a_{n}\leq i\leq b_{n}
and
\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}}:=\bigl(\widetilde{\theta}_{a_{n}}^{a_{n},b_{n}},\dotsc,\widetilde{\theta}_{b_{n}}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\bigr)
, so that
\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}}+\theta^{a_{n},b_{n}}\in\Span\{\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}},x^{a_{n},b_{n}}\}=:A^{a_{n},b_{n}}
when
\alpha>1
and
\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}}-\theta^{a_{n},b_{n}}\in A^{a_{n},b_{n}}
when
\alpha\in(0,1)
. In addition, let
\bar{x}_{a_{n},b_{n}}:=(b_{n}-a_{n}+1)^{-1}\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}x_{i}
,
so that
\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}:=x^{a_{n},b_{n}}-\bar{x}_{a_{n},b_{n}}\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}}
satisfies
\langle\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}},\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\rangle=0
and
A^{a_{n},b_{n}}=\Span\{\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}},\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\}
. Then

R_{n}=\inf_{v\in A^{a_{n},b_{n}}}\|\theta^{a_{n},b_{n}}-v\|^{2}=\inf_{v\in A^{a_{n},b_{n}}}\|\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}}-v\|^{2}=\|\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}-c_{n0}^{2}\,\|\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}-c_{n1}^{2}\,\|\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2},

(S60)

where
c_{n0}:=\langle\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}},\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\rangle/\|\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}
and
c_{n1}:=\langle\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}},\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\rangle/\|\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}
. We will consider in turn the three terms on the right-hand side of (
S60
). For each
n
, let
M_{n}:=nu_{n}
and
z_{n,i}:=(x_{i}-m_{0})/u_{n}
for
a_{n}\leq i\leq b_{n}
, where
x_{i}\equiv x_{ni}=i/n
by Assumption
2
. Then
z_{n,i+1}-z_{n,i}=1/M_{n}
for all
a_{n}\leq i<b_{n}
, and
z_{n,a_{n}}=1/2+o(1/M_{n})
and
z_{n,b_{n}}=1+o(1/M_{n})
by the definitions of
a_{n},b_{n}
. Moreover, let
\tilde{\eta}_{n}(z):=\bigl(1+\eta(u_{n}z)\bigr)^{2}-1
for
z\in[1/2,1]
and note that
\sup_{z\in[1/2,1]}\,\lvert\tilde{\eta}_{n}(z)\rvert=o(1)
as
n\to\infty
. The first term in (
S60
) can now be written as

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}B^{2}\bigl(1+\eta(x_{i}-m_{0})\bigr)^{2}(x_{i}-m_{0})^{2\alpha}

\displaystyle=nu_{n}^{2\alpha+1}\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}\frac{B^{2}}{nu_{n}}\bigl(1+\eta(x_{i}-m_{0})\bigr)^{2}\left(\frac{x_{i}-m_{0}}{u_{n}}\right)^{2\alpha}

\displaystyle=B^{2}nu_{n}^{2\alpha+1}\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}M_{n}^{-1}\bigl(1+\tilde{\eta}_{n}(z_{n,i})\bigr)\,z_{n,i}^{2\alpha}.

(S61)

Defining
F(z):=z^{\alpha}
for
z\in[1/2,1]
and noting that
M_{n}=nu_{n}=2^{-1}C_{n}(n^{2\alpha}\log n)^{1/(2\alpha+1)}\to\infty
, we have

\textstyle\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}M_{n}^{-1}z_{n,i}^{2\alpha}=\sum_{j=0}^{b_{n}-a_{n}}M_{n}^{-1}\,F(z_{n,a_{n}}+j/M_{n})^{2}=\int_{1/2}^{1}F(z)^{2}\,dz+o(1)=\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\int_{1/2}^{1}z^{2\alpha}\,dz

(S62)

as
n\to\infty
, by a Riemann sum approximation to the (uniformly) continuous function
F
on
[1/2,1]
. Since
\bigl|\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}M_{n}^{-1}\,\tilde{\eta}_{n}(z_{n,i})\,z_{n,i}^{2\alpha}\bigr|\leq\sup_{z\in[1/2,1]}\,\lvert\tilde{\eta}_{n}(z)\rvert\,\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}M_{n}^{-1}z_{n,i}^{2\alpha}=o(1)
, we deduce that

\textstyle\|\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}=B^{2}nu_{n}^{2\alpha+1}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\int_{1/2}^{1}z^{2\alpha}\,dz.

(S63)

For
\gamma\geq 0
, we can use the rescaled design points
z_{n,i}
and argue as in (
S61
) and (
S62
) to see that

\textstyle s_{n,\gamma}:=\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}(x_{i}-m_{0})^{\gamma}=nu_{n}^{\gamma+1}\textstyle\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}M_{n}^{-1}z_{n,i}^{\gamma}=nu_{n}^{\gamma+1}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\textstyle\int_{1/2}^{1}z^{\gamma}\,dz

and

\displaystyle\textstyle\tilde{s}_{n,\gamma}:=\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}\bigl(1+\eta(x_{i}-m_{0})\bigr)(x_{i}-m_{0})^{\gamma}

\displaystyle=nu_{n}^{\gamma+1}\textstyle\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}M_{n}^{-1}\bigl(1+\eta(u_{n}z_{n,i})\bigr)z_{n,i}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle=nu_{n}^{\gamma+1}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\textstyle\int_{1/2}^{1}z^{\gamma}\,dz;

see also (
S53
), (
S57
) and (
S58
) in the proof of Lemma
S5
. Now writing
x_{i}-\bar{x}_{a_{n},b_{n}}=(x_{i}-m_{0})-(\bar{x}_{a_{n},b_{n}}-m_{0})
for
a_{n}\leq i\leq b_{n}
and
\bar{x}_{a_{n},b_{n}}-m_{0}=s_{n,1}/s_{n,0}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}},\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}B\bigl(1+\eta(x_{i}-m_{0})\bigr)(x_{i}-m_{0})^{\alpha}=B\tilde{s}_{n\alpha}

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}},\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}B\bigl(1+\eta(x_{i}-m_{0})\bigr)(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{a_{n},b_{n}})(x_{i}-m_{0})^{\alpha}=B(\tilde{s}_{n,\alpha+1}-\tilde{s}_{n,\alpha}s_{n,1}/s_{n,0})

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}1=s_{n,0}

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\textstyle\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}(x_{i}-\bar{x}_{a_{n},b_{n}})^{2}=\sum_{i=a_{n}}^{b_{n}}\{(x_{i}-m_{0})^{2}-(\bar{x}_{a_{n},b_{n}}-m_{0})^{2}\}=s_{n,2}-s_{n,1}^{2}/s_{n,0}.

Setting
\bar{z}:=3/4
, we note that
s_{n,1}/s_{n,0}=u_{n}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\bar{z}
. Therefore, the second and third terms in (
S60
) can be written as

\displaystyle c_{n0}^{2}\,\|\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}},\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\rangle^{2}}{\|\mathbf{1}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{B^{2}\tilde{s}_{n,\alpha}^{2}}{s_{n,0}}=B^{2}nu_{n}^{2\alpha+1}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\!\left(\frac{\int_{1/2}^{1}z^{\alpha}\,dz}{\int_{1/2}^{1}\,dz}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle c_{n1}^{2}\,\|\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\widetilde{\theta}^{a_{n},b_{n}},\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\rangle^{2}}{\|\widetilde{x}^{a_{n},b_{n}}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{B^{2}(\tilde{s}_{n,\alpha+1}-\tilde{s}_{n,\alpha}s_{n,1}/s_{n,0})^{2}}{s_{n,2}-s_{n,1}^{2}/s_{n,0}}

\displaystyle=B^{2}nu_{n}^{2\alpha+1}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\!\left(\frac{\int_{1/2}^{1}\,(z-\bar{z})z^{\alpha}\,dz}{\int_{1/2}^{1}\,(z-\bar{z})^{2}\,dz}\right)^{2}.

(S64)

Now for
G,\tilde{G}\in L^{2}[1/2,1]
, let
\langle G,\tilde{G}\rangle_{\ast}:=\int_{1/2}^{1}G(z)\,\tilde{G}(z)\,dz
and
\|G\|_{\ast}^{2}:=\langle G,G\rangle_{\ast}
. Moreover, define
G_{0},G_{1}\colon[1/2,1]\to\mathbb{R}
by
G_{0}(z):=1
and
G_{1}(z):=z-\bar{z}
. These span the (closed) subspace
\mathcal{L}
of affine functions
G\colon[1/2,1]\to\mathbb{R}
and satisfy
\langle G_{0},G_{1}\rangle_{\ast}=0
. Let
c_{j}^{*}:=\langle F,G_{j}\rangle_{\ast}/\|G_{j}\|_{\ast}^{2}
for
j=0,1
, so that
F^{*}:=c_{0}^{*}G_{0}+c_{1}^{*}G_{1}
is the projection of
F\colon z\mapsto z^{\alpha}
onto
\mathcal{L}
with respect to
\langle\cdot\,,\cdot\rangle_{\ast}
. Since
\alpha\neq 1
in Assumption
2
, we have
F\notin\mathcal{L}
, so

\rho_{\alpha}:=\|F\|_{\ast}^{2}-c_{0}^{*}\|G_{0}\|_{\ast}^{2}-c_{1}^{*}\|G_{1}\|_{\ast}^{2}=\|F-c_{0}^{*}G_{0}-c_{1}^{*}G_{1}\|_{\ast}^{2}=\|F-F^{*}\|_{\ast}^{2}=\textstyle\int_{1/2}^{1}\,(F-F^{*})^{2}>0.

(S65)

Thus, for all sufficiently large
n
, we can combine (
S60
), (
S63
), (
S64
) and (
S65
) to conclude that

\displaystyle\inf_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{T}_{n}}\inf_{c_{0},c_{1}}\|\theta^{a,b}-c_{0}\mathbf{1}^{a,b}-c_{1}x^{a,b}\|^{2}\geq R_{n}

\displaystyle=B^{2}nu_{n}^{2\alpha+1}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\bigl(\|F\|_{\ast}^{2}-c_{0}^{*}\|G_{0}\|_{\ast}^{2}-c_{1}^{*}\|G_{1}\|_{\ast}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\rho_{\alpha}B^{2}nu_{n}^{2\alpha+1}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr).

Since
u_{n}=2^{-1}C_{n}(n/\log n)^{-1/(2\alpha+1)}=2^{-1}C_{n}\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)(n/\log n)^{-1/(2\alpha+1)}
for all
n
, this completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
S7
.

Let
h\in\mathcal{G}
be such that
h(x_{i}):=\bigl(\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\bigr)_{\tilde{k}\vee i\wedge\tilde{k}^{\prime}}
for
i\in[n]
.
Since
x_{\tilde{k}},x_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}
are successive knots of
\hat{f}_{n}^{m_{0}}\in\mathcal{F}^{m_{0}}
by assumption,
\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}=\bigl(\hat{f}_{n}^{m_{0}}(x_{\tilde{k}}),\dotsc,\hat{f}_{n}^{m_{0}}(x_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}})\bigr)
is an affine sequence. Recalling that
f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}^{m_{0}}
and
\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}=\bigl(f_{0}(x_{\tilde{k}}),\dotsc,f_{0}(x_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}})\bigr)
, we can verify that
\hat{f}_{n}^{m_{0}}+\eta h\in\mathcal{H}^{m_{0}}
for all sufficiently small
\eta>0
. Defining
\breve{\theta}:=\bigl(\hat{f}_{n}^{m_{0}}(x_{1}),\dotsc,\hat{f}_{n}^{m_{0}}(x_{n})\bigr)
,
\theta:=\bigl(f_{0}(x_{1}),\dotsc,f_{0}(x_{n})\bigr)
and
Y^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}:=(Y_{\tilde{k}},\dotsc,Y_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}})
, we deduce from (
S3
) or Lemma
S17
that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(x_{i})(Y_{i}-\breve{\theta}_{i})

\displaystyle=\bigl(\theta_{\tilde{k}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}}\bigr)\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{k}-1}\,(Y_{i}-\breve{\theta}_{i})+\langle\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}},Y^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rangle+\bigl(\theta_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\bigr)\sum_{i=\tilde{k}^{\prime}}^{n}\,(Y_{i}-\breve{\theta}_{i}).

Now since
x_{\tilde{k}},x_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}
are knots of
\hat{f}_{n}^{m_{0}}
, it follows from (
S4
) in the proof of Lemma
S1
(specialised to the setting of Proposition
S4
) that

\textstyle\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{k}-1}\,(Y_{i}-\breve{\theta}_{i})\rvert\leq\lvert Y_{\tilde{k}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}}\rvert\leq\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}}\rvert+\lvert\xi_{\tilde{k}}\rvert\;\;\;\text{and}\;\;\;\textstyle\lvert\sum_{i=\tilde{k}^{\prime}}^{n}\,(Y_{i}-\breve{\theta}_{i})\rvert\leq\lvert Y_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rvert\leq\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rvert+\lvert\xi_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rvert.

Therefore, writing
Y^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}=\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}+\xi^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}
, we have

\|\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\|^{2}\leq\langle\xi^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}},\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rangle+\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}}\rvert\,\bigl(\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}}\rvert+\lvert\xi_{\tilde{k}}\rvert\bigr)+\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rvert\,\bigl(\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rvert+\lvert\xi_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rvert\bigr).

Since
\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}}\rvert\vee\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rvert\leq\|\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\|
, this implies that

\|\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\|\leq\frac{\langle\xi^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}},\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rangle}{\|\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\|}+\lvert\xi_{\tilde{k}}\rvert+\lvert\xi_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rvert+\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}}\rvert+\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rvert.

Finally,
\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}
is an affine sequence belonging to
A_{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}=\Span\{\mathbf{1}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}},x^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\}
, so
\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\in L_{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}=\Span\{\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}},\mathbf{1}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}},x^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\}
and it follows as in (
S18
) that

\frac{\langle\xi^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}},\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rangle}{\|\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\|}\leq\|\Pi_{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\,\xi^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\|\leq\max_{1\leq a\leq b\leq n}\,\|\Pi_{a,b}\,\xi^{a,b}\|.

Since
\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}}\rvert+\lvert\theta_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\rvert\leq 2\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\,\lvert\theta_{i}-\breve{\theta}_{i}\rvert=2\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\,\lvert(\hat{f}_{n}^{m_{0}}-f_{0})(x_{i})\rvert
, we conclude that

\|\theta^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}-\breve{\theta}^{\tilde{k},\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\|\leq\max_{1\leq a\leq b\leq n}\,\|\Pi_{a,b}\,\xi^{a,b}\|+2\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\,\lvert(\hat{f}_{n}^{m_{0}}-f_{0})(x_{i})\rvert+2\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\,\lvert\xi_{i}\rvert=\Xi,

as required.
∎

Proof of Lemma
S8
.

For
\delta\in(0,(1-m_{0})/2]
and
t\in[0,(1-m_{0})/\delta]
, let

g_{0,\delta}(t):=\delta^{-\alpha}\bigl(f_{0}(m_{0})+f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})\delta t-f_{0}(m_{0}+\delta t)\bigr)\quad\text{and}\quad u_{\delta}:=\delta^{-(\alpha-1)}\bigl(f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})-u(m_{0}+\delta)\bigr),

where
\alpha>1
. Then
g_{0,\delta}
is convex and non-negative on
[0,2]
for each such
\delta
, and since
u(m_{0}+\delta)
was taken to be a subgradient of
\left.f_{0}\right|_{[m_{0},1]}
at
m_{0}+\delta
, we see that
u_{\delta}
is a subgradient of
g_{0,\delta}
at
t=1
.
Assumption
2
ensures that
g_{0,\delta}
converges uniformly on
[0,2]
to the function
g_{0}\colon t\mapsto Bt^{\alpha}
as
\delta\to 0
, and so by taking
C=(0,2)
in
Seijo and Sen 2011
, we deduce further that
u_{\delta}\to g_{0}^{\prime}(1)=B\alpha
as
\delta\to 0
.

Moreover, for
\delta\in(0,(1-m_{0})/2]
and
t\in[0,1]
, let

\displaystyle g_{1,\delta}(t)

\displaystyle:=\delta^{-\alpha}\bigl(f_{0}(m_{0})+f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})\delta t-f_{1,\delta}(m_{0}+\delta t)\bigr)

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=\delta^{-\alpha}\bigl\{\bigl(f_{0}(m_{0})+f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})\delta-f_{0}(m_{0}+\delta)\bigr)-\delta(1-t)\bigl(f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})-u(m_{0}+\delta)\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=g_{0,\delta}(1)-(1-t)u_{\delta}\leq g_{0,\delta}(t)

\displaystyle\text{and}\qquad g_{2,\delta}(t)

\displaystyle:=\delta^{-\alpha}\bigl(f_{0}(m_{0})+f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})\delta t-f_{2,\delta}(m_{0}+\delta t)\bigr)=-\delta t^{\alpha},

where
f_{1,\delta},f_{2,\delta}
are as defined in the proof of Proposition
8
. Recalling from (
S32
) that
f_{\delta}=f_{1,\delta}\wedge f_{2,\delta}
on
[m_{0},m_{0}+\delta]
by definition, we have

g_{\delta}(t):=\delta^{-\alpha}\bigl(f_{0}(m_{0})+f_{0}^{\prime}(m_{0})\delta t-f_{\delta}(m_{0}+\delta t)\bigr)=g_{1,\delta}(t)\vee g_{2,\delta}(t)

(S66)

for all
t\in[0,1]
. Note that
g_{1,\delta}(0)<g_{0,\delta}(0)=0\leq g_{2,\delta}(0)
and
g_{1,\delta}(1)=g_{0,\delta}(1)\geq 0>g_{2,\delta}(1)
. Thus, since
g_{1,\delta},g_{2,\delta}
are continuous functions that are strictly increasing and strictly decreasing respectively, there is a unique
c_{\delta}\in(0,1)
satisfying
g_{1,\delta}\leq g_{2,\delta}
on
[0,c_{\delta}]
and
g_{1,\delta}\geq g_{2,\delta}
on
[c_{\delta},1]
; in other words,
f_{1,\delta}\geq f_{2,\delta}
on
[m_{0},m_{0}+\delta c_{\delta}]
and
f_{1,\delta}\leq f_{2,\delta}
on
[m_{0}+\delta c_{\delta},m_{0}+\delta]
, so this is consistent with the definition of
c_{\delta}
in the proof of Proposition
8
. Since
g_{0,\delta}(1)-(1-c_{\delta})u_{\delta}=g_{1,\delta}(c_{\delta})=g_{2,\delta}(c_{\delta})\in[-\delta,0]
, we have

1-u_{\delta}^{-1}g_{0,\delta}(1)\geq c_{\delta}\geq 1-u_{\delta}^{-1}\bigl(g_{0,\delta}(1)+\delta\bigr).

In the limit as
\delta\to 0
, it was shown above that
g_{0,\delta}(1)\to g_{0}(1)=B
and
u_{\delta}\to g_{0}^{\prime}(1)=B\alpha
, so
c_{\delta}\to 1-\alpha^{-1}
and
g_{1,\delta}
converges uniformly on
[0,1]
to the affine function
g_{1}\colon t\mapsto g_{0}(1)-(1-t)g_{0}^{\prime}(1)=B\bigl(1-(1-t)\alpha\bigr)
. Consequently,
g_{\delta}=g_{1,\delta}\vee g_{2,\delta}\to g_{1}\vee 0\equiv g_{1}^{+}
uniformly on
[0,1]
as
\delta\to 0
.

Now let
(\delta_{n})
be any sequence such that
\delta_{n}\to 0
and
n\delta_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. Having already shown that
\lim_{n\to\infty}c_{\delta_{n}}=1-\alpha^{-1}
, we proceed to establish the claimed limiting expression for
\|f_{\delta_{n}}-f_{0}\|_{n}^{2}
. For each
n
, let
z_{n,i}:=(x_{i}-m_{0})/\delta_{n}
for
i\in[n]
, where
x_{i}\equiv x_{ni}=i/n
by Assumption
2
, so that
z_{n,i+1}-z_{n,i}=1/(n\delta_{n})
for all
i\in[n-1]
. Then recalling from (
S32
) that
f_{\delta_{n}}=f_{0}
on
[0,1]\setminus(m_{0},m_{0}+\delta_{n})
, we can use (
S66
) to write

\|f_{\delta_{n}}-f_{0}\|_{n}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\,:\,m_{0}<x_{i}<m_{0}+\delta_{n}}(f_{\delta_{n}}-f_{0})^{2}(x_{i})=\delta_{n}^{2\alpha+1}\,\frac{1}{n\delta_{n}}\sum_{i\,:\,0<z_{n,i}<1}(g_{\delta_{n}}-g_{0,\delta_{n}})^{2}(z_{n,i})

(S67)

for each
n
. Since
\lvert\{i:0<z_{n,i}<1\}\rvert=O(n\delta_{n})
and
(g_{\delta_{n}}-g_{0,\delta_{n}})^{2}\to(g_{1}^{+}-g_{0})^{2}
uniformly on
[0,1]
as
n\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\delta_{n}}\sum_{i\,:\,0<z_{n,i}<1}\lvert(g_{\delta_{n}}-g_{0,\delta_{n}})^{2}(z_{n,i})-(g_{1}^{+}-g_{0})^{2}(z_{n,i})\rvert

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq\frac{\lvert\{i:0<z_{n,i}<1\}\rvert}{n\delta_{n}}\sup_{x\in[0,1]}\bigl|(g_{\delta_{n}}-g_{0,\delta_{n}})^{2}-(g_{1}^{+}-g_{0})^{2}\bigr|=o(1).

Thus, by a Riemann sum approximation to the (uniformly) continuous function
(g_{1}^{+}-g_{0})^{2}
on
[0,1]
and the fact that
n\delta_{n}\to\infty
,
we see that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\delta_{n}}\sum_{i\,:\,0<z_{n,i}<1}(g_{\delta_{n}}-g_{0,\delta_{n}})^{2}(z_{n,i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n\delta_{n}}\sum_{i\,:\,0<z_{n,i}<1}(g_{1}^{+}-g_{0})^{2}(z_{n,i})+o(1)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\,(g_{1}^{+}-g_{0})^{2}+o(1)

\displaystyle=B^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\,\bigl\{t^{\alpha}-\bigl(1-(1-t)\alpha\bigr)^{+}\bigr\}^{2}\,dt+o(1)

\displaystyle=\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)C_{\alpha}B^{2}

as
n\to\infty
, where
C_{\alpha}:=\int_{0}^{1}\,\bigl\{t^{\alpha}-\bigl(1-(1-t)\alpha\bigr)^{+}\bigr\}^{2}\,dt
. Combining this with (
S67
) yields the desired conclusion.
∎

S6
Proofs for Section
S3

Proof of Proposition
S9
.

(a) Since every
g\in\mathcal{F}^{m}
is bounded on
[0,1]
, it follows that
\mathcal{F}^{m}\subseteq L^{2}(\nu)
. If
g,h\in\mathcal{F}^{m}
, then certainly
\lambda g\in\mathcal{F}^{m}
for all
\lambda>0
and
\lambda g+(1-\lambda)h\in\mathcal{F}^{m}
for all
\lambda\in[0,1]
. Now fix
g\in\mathcal{F}^{m}
, and for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
g_{n}\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
be the Lipschitz function that agrees with
g
on
[0,m(1-1/n)]\cup\{m\}\cup[m(1-1/n)+1/n,1]
, and is also linear on both
[m(1-1/n),m]
and
[m,m(1-1/n)+1/n]
. Then
g_{n}\in\mathcal{F}^{m}
and
g(0)\leq g_{n}\leq g(1)
for all
n
, and since
g_{n}\to g
pointwise, we have
\|g_{n}-g\|_{L^{2}(\nu)}\to 0
by the dominated convergence theorem.

(b) Let
M_{\nu}^{L}:=\min(\supp\nu)
and
M_{\nu}^{R}:=\max(\supp\nu)
, so that
\csupp\nu=[M_{\nu}^{L},M_{\nu}^{R}]
. There is nothing to prove when
m\in\csupp\nu
, so suppose now that
m>M_{\nu}^{R}
. Then
\tilde{m}=M_{\nu}^{R}
, and note that
g\colon[0,\tilde{m}]\to\mathbb{R}
is increasing, convex and Lipschitz if and only if there exists a Lipschitz
f\in\mathcal{F}^{m}
such that
g=\left.f\right|_{[0,\tilde{m}]}
. Thus,
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{m}=\{[f]_{\nu}:f\in\mathcal{F}^{\tilde{m}}\text{ is Lipschitz}\}
is dense in
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\tilde{m}}
by (a). The case
m<M_{\nu}^{L}
is similar.

(c,
\Leftarrow
) We first show that if
(f_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}
is a sequence of functions in
\mathcal{F}^{m}
such that
\|f_{n}-f\|_{L^{2}(\nu)}\to 0
for some
f\in L^{2}(\nu)
, then under any one of the conditions (i)–(iii) above, there exists
g\in\mathcal{F}^{m}
such that
f\sim_{\nu}g
. To begin with, note that
f_{n}\to f
in
\nu
-measure, so there exists a subsequence
(g_{k})_{k=1}^{\infty}\equiv(f_{n_{k}})_{k=1}^{\infty}
such that
g_{k}\to f

\nu
-almost everywhere. In each of the cases below, we will in fact show that there is some
g\in\mathcal{F}^{m}
that agrees with
f
on
A:=\{x\in\supp\nu:g_{k}(x)\to f(x)\}
, which is a dense subset of
\supp\nu
. Indeed, if
S\subseteq\supp\nu
satisfies
\nu(S^{c}\cap\supp\nu)=0
, then by the definition of
\supp\nu
, the set
S^{c}\cap\supp\nu
has empty interior; in other words,
S
is dense in
\supp\nu
.

Case 1 –
\nu([0,m))\wedge\nu((m,1])>0
: Since
A
is dense in
\supp\nu
, there exist
a_{L},a_{R}\in A\subseteq\supp\nu
such that
a_{L}<m<a_{R}
and
g_{k}\to f
on
\{a_{L},a_{R}\}
. Since the functions
g_{k}
are convex on
[0,m]
, concave on
[m,1]
and increasing on
[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{k\to\infty}g_{k}(0)

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\,\frac{m\,g_{k}(a_{L})-a_{L}\,g_{k}(m)}{m-a_{L}}\geq\frac{m\,f(a_{L})-a_{L}\,f(a_{R})}{m-a_{L}}

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\to\infty}g_{k}(1)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{k\to\infty}\,\frac{(1-m)\,g_{k}(a_{R})-(1-a_{R})\,g_{k}(m)}{a_{R}-m}\leq\frac{(1-m)\,f(a_{R})-(1-a_{R})\,f(a_{L})}{a_{R}-m},

(S68)

so
\{g_{k}(x)\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
is bounded for each
x\in[0,1]
. Therefore, by considering separately the intervals
(0,m),(m,1)
, we can apply
Rockafellar 1997
and extract a subsequence
(g_{k_{\ell}})
of
(g_{k})
that converges pointwise on
(0,m)\cup(m,1)
. In fact,
(g_{k_{\ell}})
converges pointwise on
[0,1]\setminus\{m\}
by Lemma
S29
, and the limit function
g
is convex on
[0,m)
, concave on
(m,1]
and increasing on
[0,1]\setminus\{m\}
.
If in addition
m\in A
, then

g(z)=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}g_{k_{\ell}}(z)\leq\lim_{\ell\to\infty}g_{k_{\ell}}(m)=f(m)\leq\lim_{\ell\to\infty}g_{k_{\ell}}(w)=g(w)

for all
z\in(0,m)
and
w\in(m,1)
, so
\lim_{x\nearrow\,m}g(x)\leq f(m)\leq\lim_{x\searrow\,m}g(x)
. Thus, we can extend
g
to a function on
[0,1]
that belongs to
\mathcal{F}^{m}
by setting
g(m)=f(m)
. Otherwise, if
m\notin A
, then we can set
g(m)=\lim_{x\searrow\,m}g(x)
for concreteness. In both cases, we have
g\in\mathcal{F}^{m}
and
f=g
on
A
, as required.

Case 2 –
\nu((m,1])=0
: Here, we have
\supp\nu\subseteq[0,m]
. We also assume that
\supp\nu
contains at least two points, since otherwise the result holds trivially. Note that
\conv(\Cl A)\supseteq\Int\csupp\nu=(M_{\nu}^{L},M_{\nu}^{R})
. By convexity arguments similar to those given in Case 1, it follows that
\{g_{k}(x)\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
is bounded for all
x\in(M_{\nu}^{L},M_{\nu}^{R})
.
Thus, again by
Rockafellar 1997
and Lemma
S29
, there exists a subsequence
(g_{k_{\ell}})
of
(g_{k})
that converges pointwise on
[0,M_{\nu}^{R})
to some increasing convex function
g\colon[0,M_{\nu}^{R})\to\mathbb{R}
. By the definition of
A
, we must have
f=g
on
[0,M_{\nu}^{R})\cap A
.

•

If condition (i) holds, then
M_{\nu}^{R}=m
and
\nu(\{m\})>0
, so
m\in A
, i.e.
